where
|\xi_{1}|\geq|\xi_{2}|^{a}
(notice that
|\xi_{2}|
can be small). This is the region where
N_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=\max\{|\xi_{1}|,|\xi_{2}|^{a}\}=|\xi_{1}|\geq 1
.

3.

\widehat{E}_{2}
is the set where
1\leq|\xi_{2}|
is dominant in both
\widehat{N}_{1}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
and
\widehat{N}_{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
;
i.e.
where
|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{2}|\leq|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1}|^{b}
and
|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{2}|\geq 1
. This is the region where
N_{2}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=\max\{|\xi_{1}|^{b},|\xi_{2}|\}=|\xi_{2}|\geq 1
.

The dyadic decomposition is done accordingly in each of the four regions as shown in Figure 3.1 in the case
a=1
,
b=2
.

Figure 1.
Dyadic decomposition of the frequency space
\mathbb{R}_{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}^{2}
(case
a=1
,
b=2
).

3.6.2.
Case
n=3

For the case
n=3
, let
\mathbf{E}=\big\{e(j,k)\big\}
be a standard
3\times 3
matrix. For simplicity we assume
\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}=\mathbb{R}
for
i=1,2,3
. For
1\leq i\leq 3
we define families of dilations
\widehat{\delta}^{i}_{r}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=(r^{e(1,i)}\xi_{1},r^{e(2,i)}\xi_{2},r^{e(3,i)}\xi_{3})
for
1\leq i\leq 3
. The corresponding homogeneous norms are
\widehat{N}_{i}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})=\max_{1\leq j\leq 3}|\xi_{j}|^{\frac{1}{e(j,i)}}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
. The set
\mathcal{S}_{3}
of marked partitions with three terms consists of 10 elements. Given a
3\times 3
standard matrix
\mathbf{E}
, all marked partitions give a cone
\Gamma_{S}
with nonempty interior if and only if the inequalities
e(i,k)\leq e(i,j)e(j,k)
are strict for all triples of distinct
i,j,k
.
Then the frequency space decomposes into at most
11
non-trivial regions, one of them being
B(1)
and the others associated to elements of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{E}}
.

Besides the principal marked partition
S_{p}=\{\overset{.}{1}\}\{\overset{.}{2}\}\{\overset{.}{3}\}
, we have two other types, those of the form
S=\{\overset{.}{a},b\}\{\overset{.}{c}\}
and
S=\{\overset{.}{a},b,c\}
.
We describe them with
(a,b,c)=(1,2,3)
.

1.

If
S_{p}=\{\overset{.}{1}\}\{\overset{.}{2}\}\{\overset{.}{3}\}
is the principal partition and
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\widehat{E}_{S_{p}}
then, for
1\leq i\leq 3
,
|\xi_{i}|
is dominant in
\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
, hence
\geq 1
. This is the “product” region and
\widehat{E}_{S_{p}}
is defined by the six inequalities
|\xi_{i}|\leq|\xi_{j}|^{\frac{1}{e(j,i)}}
with
i\neq j
.

2.

If
S=\{\overset{.}{1},2\}\{\overset{.}{3}\}
and
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\widehat{E}_{S}
then
|\xi_{1}|\geq 1
is dominant in
\widehat{N}_{1}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
and
\widehat{N}_{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
, while
|\xi_{3}|\geq 1
is dominant in
\widehat{N}_{3}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
. The cone
\Gamma_{S}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\mathbf{t}}
is defined by the inequalities
\frac{e(1,2)}{e(3,2)}t_{3}\leq t_{1}\leq e(1,3)t_{3}
and
t_{2}\leq\frac{1}{e(1,2)}t_{1}
. The set

\widehat{E}_{S}
is defined by the conditions
|\xi_{3}|^{\frac{e(1,2)}{e(3,2)}}\leq|\xi_{1}|\leq|\xi_{3}|^{e(1,3)}
and
N_{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=|\xi_{1}|
.

3.

If
S=\{\overset{.}{1},2,3\}
and
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\widehat{E}_{S}
then
|\xi_{1}|\geq 1
is dominant in
\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
for
1\leq i\leq 3
. The cone
\Gamma_{S}
is defined by the inequalities
t_{2}\leq\frac{t_{1}}{e(1,2)}
and
t_{3}\leq\frac{t_{1}}{e(1,3)}

and
\widehat{E}_{S}
by the single condition
N_{1}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=|\xi_{1}|\geq 1
.

\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
with the
\mathbf{t}

\Gamma_{S}
, assuming that all inequalities in (
2.4
)
e(j,\ell)<e(j,k)e(k,\ell)
with
j,k,\ell
all different are strict.

.

4.
Multi-norm Calderón reproducing formulas

In this section we develop a multi-norm version of the Calderón reproducing formula. Working in a vector space
\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
we use the following notation and terminology.

(i)

Let
r\mathbf{x}=(r^{\lambda_{1}}x_{1},\dots,r^{\lambda_{\nu}}x_{\nu})
be a one-parameter family of dilations so that
\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
has homogeneous dimension
Q=\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\lambda_{i}
. If
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{\nu}\to\mathbb{C}
and
p\in\mathbb{R}
then
\psi^{(p)}(\mathbf{x})=2^{-pQ}\psi\big(2^{-p}\mathbf{x}\big)
.

(ii)

The Schwartz norms
\|f\|_{(M)}
were defined in (
3.18
). Let
\mathcal{F}=\{f_{\sigma}:\sigma\in\Sigma\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\nu})
be a family of Schwartz functions such that
\|f_{\sigma}\|_{(M)}\leq B_{M}
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
. Then a second family
\mathcal{G}=\{g_{\sigma^{\prime}}:\sigma\in\Sigma^{\prime}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is
normalized relative to
f
if for every
M
there exists
N
so that
\|g_{\sigma^{\prime}}\|_{(M)}\leq C_{M}B_{N}
for all
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}
. (
Cf
. Definition 4.1 in
[
27
]
.)

(iii)

A function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{\nu}\to\mathbb{C}
has
cancellation of order
m

if it has vanishing moments up to order
m-1
;
i.e.

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\mathbf{x}^{{\alpha\!\!\!\!\alpha}}\psi(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=0
for all
|{\alpha\!\!\!\!\alpha}|=\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}\leq m-1
. For
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\nu})
this is the same as saying that the Fourier transform
\widehat{\psi}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
has a zero of order
m
at
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}={\mathbf{0}}
. Thus cancellation of order 1 means
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\psi(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=0
and cancellation of order 0 is a vacuous condition.

4.1.
The one-parameter case

Let
\big\{\varphi_{\ell}:\ell\in\mathbb{N}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\nu})
be a sequence that is uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm and satisfies
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\varphi_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=1
for
\ell\geq 0
. Put
\varphi_{-1}(\mathbf{x})\equiv 0
. Then define

\displaystyle\psi_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})=\varphi_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})-\varphi_{\ell-1}^{(1)}(\mathbf{x})\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\psi_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\ell=0$}\\
0&\text{if $\ell\neq 0$}\end{cases}.

(4.1)

Then

\displaystyle\psi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=2^{\ell Q}\psi_{\ell}(2^{\ell}\mathbf{x})-2^{(\ell-1)Q}\psi_{\ell-1}(2^{\ell-1}\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\text{so that }

\displaystyle\varphi_{N}^{(-N)}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{N}\psi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}(\mathbf{x})\quad\forall N\geq 0.

Then in the sense of distributions,

\displaystyle\delta_{{\mathbf{0}}}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{\ell\geq 0}\psi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle\delta_{{\mathbf{0}}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{M}^{(-M)}+\sum\nolimits_{\ell>M}\psi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}

\displaystyle\text{ for any $M\geq 0$}.

As a consequence of Hadamard’s lemma we have

Lemma 4.1
.

A function
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\nu})
has cancellation of order
m
if and only if there exist functions
\big\{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\nu}):|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m\big\}
such that
\psi=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\psi_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
with
\big\{\psi_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big\}
normalized relative to
\psi
.

The following version of the Calderón reproducing formula is taken from
[
33
]
. The proof given below is simplified by the use of Fourier transform and requires weaker assumptions. Let
B({\mathbf{0}},r)
be the open ball centered at the origin with radius
r
. Denote by
\widehat{g}
or
\mathcal{F}[g]
the Fourier transform of a function
g
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\{\varphi_{\ell}\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\nu})
be uniformly bounded with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\varphi_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=1
, and let
\psi_{\ell}
be defined in (
4.1
). Given
m>0
there are two families of Schwartz functions,
\{\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\{\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
,
such that

(a)

the families
\{\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}\}
and
\{\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}\}
are normalized relative to
\{\varphi_{\ell}\}
;

(b)

\widetilde{\psi}_{0}=\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}
;

(c)

for
\ell>0
,
\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}
has cancellation of order
m
;

(d)

if
\ell\geq 0
then
\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{0}}=\varphi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}*\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}+\sum\nolimits_{\ell^{\prime}>\ell}\psi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}
;

(e)

if
\ell\geq 0
then
\displaystyle\varphi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}*\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}=\sum\nolimits_{0\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell}\psi_{\ell^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}
;

(f)

if the Fourier transforms
\mathcal{F}[\varphi_{\ell}]
are supported on
B({\mathbf{0}},r)

and are identically equal to
1
on
B\left({\mathbf{0}},\frac{r}{2}\right)
so that
\{\psi_{\ell}\}
are supported on
B({\mathbf{0}},2r)\setminus B\left({\mathbf{0}},\frac{r}{2}\right)
, then the Fourier transforms
\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}]
are supported on
B({\mathbf{0}},r)
and
\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}]
are supported on
B({\mathbf{0}},2r)\setminus B\left({\mathbf{0}},\frac{r}{2}\right)
;

(g)

if the functions
\{\varphi_{\ell}\}
are supported on a compact neighborhood
K
of
{\mathbf{0}}
, then the functions
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell},\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}
are supported on a dilate
c_{m}K
where
c_{m}
depends only on
m
.

Proof.

The hypothesis on the
\varphi_{\ell}
imply that
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathcal{F}[\varphi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}]=1
point-wise and boundedly, so that
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\varphi_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}=\delta_{\mathbf{0}}
. The same is true with
\varphi_{\ell}
replaced by
g_{\ell}=\varphi_{\ell}*\varphi_{\ell}
. Let
\nu_{\ell}=\mathcal{F}[\varphi_{\ell}]
and
\mu_{\ell}=\mathcal{F}[\psi_{\ell}]
, so that
\mu_{0}=\nu_{0}
and
\mu_{\ell}(\xi)=\mathcal{F}[\psi_{\ell}](\xi)=\mathcal{F}[\varphi_{\ell}](\xi)-\mathcal{F}[\varphi_{\ell-1}](2\xi)
for
\ell>0
. By dominated convergence, we have the following identities in the sense of distributions for all
M\geq 1
:

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathcal{F}[g_{\ell}^{(-\ell)}]({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\nu_{\ell}^{2}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})+\sum\nolimits_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\big(\nu_{\ell}^{2}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})-\nu_{\ell-1}^{2}(2^{-\ell+1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)

\displaystyle=\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})+\sum\nolimits_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\mu_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big(\nu_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})+\nu_{\ell-1}(2^{-\ell+1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})+\sum\nolimits_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\mu_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big(\nu_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})+\nu_{\ell-1}(2^{-\ell+1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)\Big)^{M}=\big(\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})+s_{0}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{M}\ ,

We expand the
M
-th power into terms depending on
(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{M})\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
and group together all terms with the same
\bar{\ell}=\min\{\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{M}\}
.
For
\bar{\ell}=0
we must take all terms containing
\nu_{0}
, which add up to
1-s_{0}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{M}=1-\big(1-\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{M}=\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\sum_{k=0}^{M-1}\big(1-\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{k}
, since
s_{0}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=1-\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
.

We then define
\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}=\widetilde{\psi}_{0}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big[\nu_{0}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\sum_{k=0}^{M-1}\big(1-\nu_{0}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{k}\big]
, and for any
\bar{\ell}>0
, we denote by
s_{\bar{\ell}}
the remainder
s_{\bar{\ell}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\sum_{\ell=\bar{\ell}}^{\infty}\big(\nu_{\ell}^{2}(2^{-\ell}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})-\nu_{\ell-1}^{2}(2^{-\ell+1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)=1-\nu_{\bar{\ell}-1}^{2}(2^{-\bar{\ell}+1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})
. The terms to be grouped together are
those appearing in the expansion of
s_{\bar{\ell}}^{M}
but not in the expansion of
s_{\bar{\ell}+1}^{M}
. These are

\displaystyle s_{\bar{\ell}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{M}-s_{\bar{\ell}+1}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{M}

\displaystyle=\big(1-\nu_{\bar{\ell}-1}^{2}(2^{-\bar{\ell}+1}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{M}-\big(1-\nu_{\bar{\ell}}^{2}(2^{-\bar{\ell}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{M}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\bar{\ell}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big(\nu_{\bar{\ell}}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})+\nu_{{\bar{\ell}}-1}(2{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)\sum\nolimits_{k=0}^{M-1}\big(1-\nu_{{\bar{\ell}}-1}^{2}(2{\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{M-1-k}\big(1-\nu_{\bar{\ell}}^{2}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\big)^{k}\ .

For
\ell\geq 1
define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\nu_{\ell}\sum\nolimits_{k=0}^{M-1}\big(1-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\big)^{k}\Big)

(4.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big(\big(\nu_{\ell}+\nu_{\ell-1}(2\cdot)\big)\sum\nolimits_{k=0}^{M-1}\big(1-\nu_{\ell-1}^{2}(2\cdot)\big)^{M-1-k}\big(1-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\big)^{k}\Big)\ ,

so that

\displaystyle\qquad\varphi_{\ell}*\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathbf{0}}-(\delta_{\mathbf{0}}-g_{\ell}\big)^{*M}

(4.3)

\displaystyle\psi_{\ell}*\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\big(\delta_{\mathbf{0}}-g_{\ell-1}^{(1)}\big)^{*M}-\big(\delta_{\mathbf{0}}-g_{\ell}\big)^{*M}\ ,

where the exponent
*M
denotes
M
-th convolution power. It is quite clear that
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}
are Schwartz functions.
Then (
4.2
) shows uniform boundedness of their Schwartz norms, as well as cancellation of order
M-1
of
\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}
at
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}={\mathbf{0}}
, while (
4.3
) provides the identities
\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}\xi)\widetilde{\mu}_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}\xi)=1
and
\sum_{0\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell}\mu_{\ell^{\prime}}(2^{-\ell^{\prime}}\xi)\widetilde{\mu}_{\ell^{\prime}}(2^{-\ell^{\prime}}\xi)=\nu_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}\xi)\widetilde{\nu}_{\ell}(2^{-\ell}\xi)
. This establishes (
d
) and (
e
). Then (
f
) follows from (
4.2
). Also (
g
) follows from (
4.2
), observing that
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\ell}
is a linear combination of positive convolution powers of
\varphi_{\ell}
, up to degree
2M+1
. The same applies to
\widetilde{\psi}_{\ell}
.
∎

4.2.
Multi-norm Calderón reproducing formulas by tensor products

Based on the formulas in Section
4.1
, we now construct a multi-norm Calderón reproducing formula adapted to the dyadic decomposition from Section
3.3
. Let
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{n}}
, and for
1\leq i\leq n
let
\big\{\varphi_{i,\ell}:\ell\geq 0\big\}\subset\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
be a family of functions uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm satisfying
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\varphi_{i,\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{i})d\mathbf{x}_{i}=1
. Let
\displaystyle\psi_{i,\ell}(\mathbf{x})=\varphi_{i,\ell}(\mathbf{x})-\varphi_{i,\ell-1}^{(1)}(\mathbf{x})
, and let
\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell},\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell}
be the functions constructed from
\varphi_{i,\ell}
and
\psi_{i,\ell}
as in Lemma
4.2
. Let
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}(D)\subset\mathbb{N}^{n}
be the index sets defined in equation (
3.14
).
If
L\in\mathcal{L}
recall that
D_{L}
is the unique subset of
\{1,\ldots,n\}
such that
L\in D_{L}
. For
L=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n})\in\mathcal{L}
let

\displaystyle\Phi_{L}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\prod\nolimits_{i\in D_{L}}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\prod\nolimits_{i\notin D_{L}}\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),

(4.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in D_{L}}\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\prod_{i\notin D_{L}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),

\displaystyle\Psi_{i,L}(\mathbf{x}_{i})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $i\in D_{L}$}\\
\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $i\notin D_{L}$}\end{cases},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{i,L}(\mathbf{x}_{i})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $i\in D_{L}$}\\
\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $i\notin D_{L}$}\end{cases}.

We say that the family
\Psi=\big\{\Phi_{L},\Psi_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
is
constructed via tensor products
from
\big\{\varphi_{i,\ell}\big\}
. If
L\in\mathcal{L}_{D}
and
i\in D
then
\ell_{i}>0
so
\Psi_{L}(\mathbf{x})
has integral zero in
\mathbf{x}_{i}
, and
\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}(\mathbf{x})
has cancellation of order
m
in
\mathbf{x}_{i}
. On the Fourier transform side,
\mathcal{F}[\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}]
and
\mathcal{F}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}]
give a Schwartz partition of unity subordinated to the sets
B_{L}=\tau^{-1}(T_{L})
where
T_{L}
is the rectangle in (
3.15
). We have the following multi-norm reproducing formulas.

Proposition 4.3
.

(a)

The identities
\delta_{\mathbf{0}}=\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}
and
\delta_{\mathbf{0}}=\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}
hold in the sense of distributions.

(b)

If
L,L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
write
L^{\prime}\leq L
if and only if
\ell^{\prime}_{i}\leq\ell_{i}
for all
i
and
L^{\prime}<L
if and only if
L^{\prime}\leq L
and
L^{\prime}\neq L
. Then for every
L\in\mathcal{L}
,
\Phi_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}^{(-L)}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}\,,\,L^{\prime}\leq L}\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}
.

Proof.

We only prove the last identity since the first two are analogous and simpler.
According to Lemma
4.2
(
e
),
\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell)}=\sum\nolimits_{0\leq\ell_{i}^{\prime}\leq\ell_{i}}\psi_{{i,\ell_{i}}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{{i,\ell_{i}}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}
. Thus

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}

\displaystyle*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\in D_{L}}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\notin D_{L}}\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)*\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\in D_{L}}\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\notin D_{L}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\in D_{L}}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\notin D_{L}}\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\in D_{L}}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\notin D_{L}}\sum\nolimits_{0\leq\ell_{i}^{\prime}\leq\ell_{i}}\psi_{{i,\ell_{i}}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{{i,\ell_{i}}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in T_{L}}\mathop{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}(\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})})(\mathbf{x}).

Then by Lemma
4.2

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}\mathop{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}\big(\psi_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}\big)=\mathop{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}\sum\nolimits_{\ell^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}}\big(\psi_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}\big)=\delta_{\mathbf{0}},

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}\\
L^{\prime}\leq L\end{subarray}}\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\\
L^{\prime}\leq L\end{subarray}}\mathop{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}\big(\psi_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}\big)=\mathop{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell_{i}^{\prime}\leq\ell_{i}}\big(\psi_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}\big)=\Phi_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}^{(-L)}.\qed

4.3.
Multi-norm Calderón reproducing formulas by convolution

In this section we construct reproducing formulas
\delta_{{\mathbf{0}}}=\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}
and
\delta_{{\mathbf{0}}}=\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L}^{(-L)}
where the functions
\big\{\Sigma_{L}\big\}
are convolutions rather than tensor products. Recall from (
2.5
) that
\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})
is a homogeneous norm for the dilations
\widehat{\delta}^{i}_{r}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\left(r^{e(1,i)}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,r^{e(n,i)}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}\right)
. If
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
let
f^{(-t)_{i}}
denote
f
rescaled by
2^{-t}
relative to the dilations
\widehat{\delta}^{i}_{r}
. Thus
f^{(-t)_{i}}(\mathbf{x})=2^{t\widehat{Q}_{i}}f\big(\widehat{\delta}^{i}_{2^{t}}(\mathbf{x})\big)
, where
\widehat{Q}_{i}
, defined in (
3.19
), denotes the homogeneous dimension of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
relative to these dilations.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathbf{t}=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
and let
\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}=(\widetilde{t}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{t}_{n})
where
\widetilde{t}_{j}=\min_{1\leq i\leq n}e(j,i)t_{i}
. Then
\widetilde{t}_{j}\leq t_{j}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}\in\Gamma(\mathbf{E})
. Moreover
\mathbf{t}\in\Gamma(\mathbf{E})
if and only if
\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}=\mathbf{t}
.

Proof.

Since
e(j,j)=1
it follows that
\widetilde{t}_{j}\leq t_{j}
. For any
i,j,k
we have
\widetilde{t}_{j}\leq e(j,i)t_{i}\leq e(j,k)e(k,i)t_{i}
. Taking the minimum over
i
it follows that
\widetilde{t}_{j}\leq e(j,k)\widetilde{t}_{k}
and so
\widetilde{t}\in\Gamma(\mathbf{E})
. Finally,
\mathbf{t}\in\Gamma(\mathbf{E})
if and only if
t_{j}=\min_{1\leq i\leq n}e(j,i)t_{i}=\widetilde{t}_{j}
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathbf{t}=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
and let
h\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be such that
f_{1}^{(-t_{1})_{1}}*\cdots*f_{n}^{(-t_{n})_{n}}=h^{(-\widetilde{\mathbf{t}})}
where
\widetilde{\mathbf{t}}
is given in Lemma
4.4
. Then
\|h\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{(N^{\prime})}
for every
N\geq 0
where
C_{N}
and
N^{\prime}
depend only on
N
.

Proof.

If
h=\big(f_{1}^{(-t_{1})_{1}}*\cdots*f_{n}^{(-t_{n})_{n}}\big)^{(\widetilde{\mathbf{t}})}
then
\mathcal{F}[h](\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{F}\big[f_{1}^{(-t_{1})_{1}}*\cdots*f_{n}^{(-t_{n})_{n}}\big](2^{\widetilde{t}_{1}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\ldots,2^{\widetilde{t}_{n}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n})=\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{f_{i}}\big(2^{\tilde{t}_{1}-e(1,i)t_{i}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1},\dots,2^{\tilde{t}_{n}-e(n,i)t_{i}}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}\big)
. We show that all derivatives of
\mathcal{F}[h]
are rapidly decaying at infinity. Since
\tilde{t}_{j}-e(j,i)t_{i}\leq 0
, using Leibniz formula it suffices to prove the inequality

\displaystyle\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\tilde{t}_{1}-e(1,i)t_{i}}|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1}|+\cdots+2^{\tilde{t}_{n}-e(n,i)t_{i}}|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}|\big)\geq 1+|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1}|+\cdots+|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}|.

(4.5)

Let
E_{i}
the set of
j
such that
\tilde{t}_{j}=e(j,i)t_{i}
. Then, since every
j
is in some
E_{i}
,

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\tilde{t}_{1}-e(1,i)t_{i}}|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{1}|+\cdots+2^{\tilde{t}_{n}-e(n,i)t_{i}}|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{n}|\big)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1+\sum_{j\in E_{i}}|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}|\Big)\geq 1+\sum_{j=1}^{n}|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{j}|.\qed

We give a discrete version of Lemma
4.5
involving only dyadic scales
2^{-L}
with
L\in\mathcal{L}
.
If
|\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{t}^{\prime}|<\delta
, if
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and if
f^{(-\mathbf{t})}=g^{(-\mathbf{t}^{\prime})}
then
g\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\|g\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N,\delta}\|f\|_{(N)}
for
\forall N
.

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
L=(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{n})\in\mathcal{L}
and
f_{1},\dots,f_{n}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then
f_{1}^{(-\ell_{1})_{1}}*\cdots*f_{n}^{(-\ell_{n})_{n}}=k^{(-L)}
where
k\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\|k\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{(N^{\prime})}
for every
N\geq 0
, with
C_{N}
and
N^{\prime}
depend only on
N
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
,
\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}=h^{(-\mathbf{t}_{L})}
where
\mathbf{t}_{L}
has entries
t_{j}=\min_{i=1,\dots,n}e(j,i)\ell_{i}
and
\|h\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{(N^{\prime})}
. By Lemma
3.9
(
i
) there is
\mathbf{t}^{\prime}\in\Gamma({\mathbf{E}})
such that
|\mathbf{t}^{\prime}-L|<\kappa
. Given
j
, let
i
be such that
t_{j}=e(j,i)\ell_{i}
. Then
t_{j}\leq\ell_{j}
and

|\ell_{j}-t_{j}|=\ell_{j}-e(j,i)\ell_{i}\leq t^{\prime}_{j}+\kappa-e(j,i)(t^{\prime}_{i}-\kappa)\leq\big(1+e(j,i)\big)\kappa\ .

Hence the distances
|L-\mathbf{t}_{L}|
are uniformly bounded and we can define
k=\big[h^{(-\mathbf{t}_{L})}\big]^{(L)}
, keeping the uniform control of Schwartz norms.
∎

Now for
1\leq i\leq n
fix a family
\{\rho_{i,\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm and with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{i,\ell}=1
. Following the procedure in Subsection
4.1
, relative to the
\widehat{\delta}^{i}_{r}
dilations in (
2.5
), construct the functions
\sigma_{i,\ell}=\rho_{i,\ell}-\rho_{i,\ell-1}^{(1)_{i}}
as in (
4.1
) and let
\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\ell},\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell}
be the functions, depending on the order
m
of the required cancellations, given by Lemma
4.2
.
We define the functions
\Sigma_{L}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L}
by

\displaystyle\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathop{*}_{i\in D_{L}}\sigma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\Big)*\Big(\mathop{*}_{i\not\in D_{L}}\rho_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L}^{(-L)}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathop{*}_{i\in D_{L}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\Big)*\Big(\mathop{*}_{i\not\in D_{L}}\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\Big)

(4.6)

where
D_{L}
is such that
L\in\Gamma(D_{L})
.
The following statement is the analogue of Proposition
4.3
.

Proposition 4.7
.

We have

\delta_{\mathbf{0}}=\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}
and
\delta_{\mathbf{0}}=\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L}^{(-L)}
in the sense of distributions. Also if
{\rm P}_{L}=\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{i,\ell_{i}}
and
\widetilde{\rm P}_{L}=\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\rho}_{i,\ell_{i}}
, then
{\rm P}_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\rm P}_{L}^{(-L)}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}\,,\,L^{\prime}\leq L}\Sigma_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}
.

Proof.

The identity
\delta_{\mathbf{0}}=\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}\Big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\Big)*\Big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\Big)
is a trivial consequences of Lemma
4.2
(
d
). By Lemma
3.7
(
b
),
\mathbb{N}^{n}
is the disjoint union of the sets
T_{L}\cap\mathbb{N}^{n}
, parametrized by
L\in\mathcal{L}
. For
L\in\mathcal{L}_{D}
, with
D\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
,
T_{L}\cap\mathbb{N}^{n}
consists of the elements
L^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
such that

\ell^{\prime}_{i}=\ell_{i}
if
i\in D
and
\ell^{\prime}_{i}\leq\ell_{i}
if
i\not\in D
. It follows from Lemma
4.2
(
e
) that

\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L}^{(-L)}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\in T_{L}\cap\mathbb{N}^{n}}\big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}\big)*\big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}\big)
for every
L\in\mathcal{L}
. This gives the two reproducing formulas. The proof of the formula for
{\rm P}_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\rm P}_{L}^{(-L)}
is similar.
∎

We say that the family
\Sigma=\big\{\Sigma_{L},{\rm P}_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
is
constructed via convolution
from
\big\{\rho_{i,\ell}\big\}
.

4.4.
Associated square functions

Definition 4.8
.

All reproducing formulas defined above have corresponding square functions.

(i)

Let
\Psi=\big\{\Psi_{L},\Phi_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
be a family constructed via tensor product. The associated
square function of tensor type
is

S_{\Psi}f=\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.7)

(ii)

Let
\Sigma=\big\{\Sigma_{L},{\rm P}_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
be a family constructed via convolution. The associated
square function of convolution type
is

S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f=\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(4.8)

The main result of the next two section is that, for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the condition
Sf\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is independent of the choice of
the square function
S
among those defined above. We call this property
L^{1}
-equivalence
.

5.
L^{1}
-equivalence of multi-norm square functions of tensor type

Let
\Psi=\big\{\Phi_{L},\Psi_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
and
\Psi^{\prime}=\big\{\Phi^{\prime}_{L},\Psi^{\prime}_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
be two families constructed via tensor products from
\big\{\varphi_{i,\ell}\big\}
and
\big\{\varphi^{\prime}_{i,\ell}\big\}
respectively and let
S_{\Psi}
,
S_{\Psi^{\prime}}
be the associated square functions.

Theorem 5.1
.

If
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then
S_{\Psi}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
S_{\Psi^{\prime}}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. More precisely, there is a constant
C>0
independent of
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
and an integer
M>0
depending on the exponents
\{\lambda_{i}\}
and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
so that if
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
S_{\Psi^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then

\big|\big|S_{\Psi^{\prime}}f\big|\big|_{L^{1}}\leq C\,\big|\big|\Psi^{\prime}\big|\big|_{(M)}\big|\big|S_{\Psi}f\big|\big|_{L^{1}},

(5.1)

and similarly with
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
interchanged.

The proof is modelled upon
[
11
]
and is based on the existence of associated Calderón reproducing formulas, cf. Proposition
4.3
(a), involving auxiliary functions
\widetilde{\Psi}_{L},\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}
, which are also tensor products of function
\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}},\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}}
, and
\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}
can be assumed, for
i\in D_{L}
, to have cancellations of order
m
in the variables
\mathbf{x}_{i}
, with
m
arbitrarily large. We also need several lemmas.
Recall that
\big\{\lambda_{i}:1\leq i\leq d\big\}
are the exponents of the dilations in equation (
1.1
).
Let

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}_{i}

\displaystyle=\min_{k\in E_{i}}\lambda_{k}

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\min_{1\leq i\leq d}\lambda_{i}.

(5.2)

If
f,g\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}^{d_{i}}
,

\displaystyle(f*g)^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=f^{(\ell)}*g^{(\ell)},

\displaystyle\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big[f*g\big]

\displaystyle=\big[\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f\big]*g=f*\big[\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}g\big],

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle\big[\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f\big]^{(-\ell)}

\displaystyle=2^{-\ell\,[\![\boldsymbol{\beta}]\!]_{i}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big[f^{(-\ell)}\big].

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
f,g\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
, and suppose
f
has cancellation of order
m
and
g
has cancellation of order
m^{\prime}
. Then for any
p,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
h\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
with order of cancellation
m+m^{\prime}
and normalized relative to
f
and
g
so that

\displaystyle f^{(-p)}*g^{(-\ell)}=\begin{cases}2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m(p-\ell)}h^{(-\ell)}&\text{ if $p\geq\ell$}\\
2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m^{\prime}(\ell-p)}h^{(-p)}&\text{ if $\ell\geq p$}\end{cases}.

Proof.

We can assume
p\geq\ell
. Also
f^{(-p)}*g^{(-\ell)}=\big(f^{(-(p-\ell))}*g\big)^{(-\ell)}
so by rescaling we can assume that
\ell=0
. Note that
[\![\beta]\!]_{i}=\sum_{k\in E_{i}}\beta_{k}\lambda_{k}\geq\bar{\lambda}_{i}\sum_{k\in E_{i}}\beta_{k}=m\bar{\lambda}_{i}
so
2^{-\ell\,[[\![\boldsymbol{\beta}]\!]_{i}-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m]}\leq 1
. By Lemma
4.1
we have
f=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
. Thus

\displaystyle f^{(-p)}*g

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}\big[\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big]^{(-p)}*g=\sum\nolimits_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}2^{-p\,[\![\boldsymbol{\beta}]\!]_{i}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big[f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{(-p)}\big]*g

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}2^{-p\,[\![\boldsymbol{\beta}]\!]_{i}}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big[f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{(-p)}*g\big]=2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m\ell}\Big[\sum\nolimits_{|\boldsymbol{\beta}|=m}2^{-\ell\,[[\![\boldsymbol{\beta}]\!]_{i}-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m]}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big[f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{(-p)}*g\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=:2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m\ell}h.

Clearly
h
has vanishing moments up to order
m+m^{\prime}
. To estimates the Schwartz norms of
\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big[f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{(-\mathbf{p})}*g\big]=f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{(-p)}*\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}}g
we must estimate
(1+|\mathbf{t}|)^{N}\big|\mathbf{f}_{\beta}^{(-p)}*\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}g(\mathbf{t})\big|
for
\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
.

\displaystyle(1

\displaystyle+|\mathbf{t}|)^{N}\big|\mathbf{f}_{\beta}^{(-p)}*\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}g(\mathbf{t})\big|=(1+|\mathbf{t}|)^{N}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}2^{q_{i}p}f_{\beta}(2^{p}\mathbf{s})\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}g(\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{s})\,d\mathbf{s}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{(N)}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}g\|_{(N)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\frac{\big(1+|\mathbf{t}|\big)^{N}2^{q_{i}\ell}d\mathbf{s}}{\big(1+\big|2^{\ell}\mathbf{s}\big|\big)^{N}\big(1+|\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{s}|\big)^{N}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{N}\|f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{(N)}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}g\|_{(N)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\frac{\big|2^{\ell}\mathbf{s}\big|^{N}+2^{q_{i}\ell}\big(1+|\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{s}|\big)^{N}}{\big(1+\big|2^{\ell}\mathbf{s}\big|\big)^{N}\big(1+|\mathbf{t}-\mathbf{s}|\big)^{N}}\,d\mathbf{s}\leq C_{N}\|f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{(N)}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\beta}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}g\|_{(N)}.\qed

For
1\leq i\leq n
let
\{\varphi_{i,\ell}:\ell\in\mathbb{N}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
and
\{\varphi_{i,p}^{\prime}:p\in\mathbb{N}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
be uniformly bounded families with
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\varphi_{i,\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\,d\mathbf{x}_{i}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\varphi_{i,p}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\,d\mathbf{x}_{i}=1
. Let
\Psi=\big\{\Psi_{L},\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
and
\Psi^{\prime}=\big\{\Psi_{P}^{\prime},\widetilde{\Psi}_{P}^{\prime}:P\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
be the families of Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
constructed via tensor products from
\{\varphi_{i,\ell}\}
and
\{\varphi_{i,p}^{\prime}\}
as in Section
4.2
. If
P,L\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
then
|P-L|=\max_{i=1,\dots,n}|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|
. Let
\kappa
be the constant from Lemma
3.9
and let
N=\kappa\big(1+\max_{j,k}e(j,k)^{-1}\big)
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
M>0
and let
m
be the order of cancellation of the functions
\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell}
. With
\bar{\lambda}
defined in (
5.2
), if
\bar{\lambda}(m-1)>M
there exists
C,\epsilon>0
depending only on
\kappa
,
\bar{\lambda}
, and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
so that for all
P=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})
and
L=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n})
in
\mathcal{L}
we have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1+2^{\ell_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|)\big)^{M}\big|(\Psi_{P}^{\prime})^{(-P)}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L})^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}\leq C\,2^{-\epsilon|P-L|}
.

Proof.

Let
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}
and
T=\big\{(B_{1},m_{1}),\ldots,(B_{\sigma},m_{\sigma})\big\}
be marked partitions such that
P\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
and
L\in\mathcal{L}_{T}
. Put
D_{S}=\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{s}\}
and
D_{T}=\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{\sigma}\}
. Since
\Psi_{P}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}
are tensor products the integral in the statement equals
\prod_{i=1}^{n}I_{i}
where

\displaystyle I_{i}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\big(1+2^{\ell_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)^{M}\big|(\psi_{i,p_{i}}^{\prime})^{(-p_{i})}*(\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}})^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}_{i}.

We can assume that
|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|>\kappa(1+\max_{j,k}e(j,k)^{-1})
for otherwise
2^{-\bar{\lambda}|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|}
is controlled from above and below by fixed constants and the inequality
I_{i}\leq C2^{-\varepsilon|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|}
is obvious.
By Lemma
5.2
there exists
h_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
, normalized relative to
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
, so that

\displaystyle(\Psi_{i,P}^{\prime})^{(-p_{i})}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{i,L})^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=\begin{cases}2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}(p_{i}-\ell_{i})}h_{i}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $p_{i}\geq\ell_{i}$ and $i\in D_{S}$}\\
h_{i}^{(-\ell_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $p_{i}\geq\ell_{i}$ and $i\notin D_{S}$}\\
2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m(\ell_{i}-p_{i})}h_{i}^{(-p_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $\ell_{i}\geq p_{i}$ and $i\in D_{T}$}\\
h_{i}^{(-p_{i})}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $\ell_{i}\geq p_{i}$ and $i\notin D_{T}$}\end{cases}

and so

\displaystyle I_{i}\leq\begin{cases}C_{M}\,2^{-\bar{\lambda}(p_{i}-\ell_{i})}&\text{if $p_{i}\geq\ell_{i}$ and $i\in D_{S}$}\\
C_{M}&\text{if $p_{i}\geq\ell_{i}$ and $i\notin D_{S}$}\\
C_{M}\,2^{-(\bar{\lambda}m-M)(\ell_{i}-p_{i})}&\text{if $\ell_{i}\geq p_{i}$ and $i\in D_{T}$}\\
C_{M}\,2^{M(\ell_{i}-p_{i})}&\text{if $\ell_{i}\geq p_{i}$ and $i\notin D_{T}$}\end{cases}.

(5.3)

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{+}

\displaystyle=\Big\{i:p_{i}>\ell_{i},\,i\in D_{S}\Big\}

\displaystyle\text{\qquad and \quad}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{+}

\displaystyle=\Big\{i:\ell_{i}>p_{i},\,i\in D_{T}\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{-}

\displaystyle=\Big\{i:p_{i}>\ell_{i},\,i\notin D_{S}\Big\}

\displaystyle\text{\qquad and \quad}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{-}

\displaystyle=\Big\{i:\ell_{i}>p_{i},\,i\notin D_{T}\Big\}.

The estimates (
5.3
) are small in
|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|
only when
i\in\mathcal{S}^{+}
, in which case
I_{i}\leq C_{M}e^{-\bar{\lambda}(p_{i}-\ell_{i})}
, or when
i\in\mathcal{T}^{+}
, in which case
I_{i}\leq C_{M}2^{-(\bar{\lambda}m-M)(\ell_{i}-p_{i})}
. If
i\in\mathcal{S}^{-}
the estimate is not small and if
i\in\mathcal{T}^{-}
the estimate for
I_{i}
is large. To prove Lemma
5.3
we show that we can “borrow” some of the gain from
I_{i}
when
i\in\mathcal{S}^{+}\cup\mathcal{T}^{+}
to obtain a gain for
I_{j}
when
j\notin\mathcal{S}^{+}\cup\mathcal{T}^{+}
. We show that there are constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
depending only on
\kappa
and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
so that if
|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|>\kappa\big(1+\max_{j,k}e(j,k)^{-1}\big)
then the following hold.

(1)

If
i\in\mathcal{S}^{-}
there exists
k_{r}\in\mathcal{S}^{+}
with
p_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}>c_{1}(p_{i}-\ell_{i})-c_{2}
, and so for
0<\alpha<\!\!<1

\displaystyle I_{k_{r}}^{\alpha}I_{i}\leq C_{M}2^{-\bar{\lambda}\alpha(p_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}})}\leq C_{M}^{\prime}2^{-\bar{\lambda}c_{1}\alpha(p_{i}-\ell_{i})}.

(2)

If
i\in\mathcal{T}^{-}
there exists
m_{q}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}
with
\ell_{m_{q}}-p_{m_{q}}\geq c_{1}(\ell_{i}-p_{i})-c_{2}
, and so

\displaystyle I_{m_{q}}^{\alpha}I_{i}\leq C_{M}2^{M-(\bar{\lambda}m-M)\alpha(\ell_{m_{q}}-p_{m_{q}})}\leq C_{M}^{\prime}2^{M-(\bar{\lambda}m-M-1)c_{1}\alpha(\ell_{i}-p_{i})}.

Assuming (
1
) and (
2
), then choosing
\alpha
sufficiently small and then
m
sufficiently large, it follows that there exists
\epsilon>0
depending on
n
,
\kappa
, and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
so that
\prod_{i=1}^{n}I_{i}\leq C_{M}^{\prime\prime}2^{-\epsilon|P-L|}
.

Before proving (
1
) and (
2
), it follows from Lemma
3.9
that since
P\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
and
L\in\mathcal{L}_{T}
, there are points
\mathbf{u}\in F_{S}
and
\mathbf{v}\in F_{T}
with
|P-\mathbf{u}|\leq\kappa
and
|L-\mathbf{v}|\leq\kappa
. Since
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma(\mathbf{E})
we have
u_{i}\leq e(i,j)u_{j}
and
v_{i}\leq e(i,j)v_{j}
for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
. Also
i\in A_{r}\Longrightarrow p_{i}\leq\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}
and
i\in B_{q}\Longrightarrow m_{i}\leq\frac{\ell_{m_{q}}}{e(m_{q},i)}
.

Proof of
(1)
.

Suppose
i\in A_{r}
so
p_{i}\leq\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}
. Note that
i\notin\mathcal{T}^{+}
since otherwise
\ell_{i}>p_{i}
. We have
\ell_{i}=v_{i}+(\ell_{i}-v_{i})
with
|\ell_{i}-v_{i}|\leq\kappa
. Since
\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma(\mathbf{E})
we have
v_{k_{r}}\leq e(k_{r},i)v_{i}
so

\displaystyle p_{i}-\ell_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}-\ell_{i}=\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}-v_{i}+|v_{i}-\ell_{i}|\leq\frac{p_{k_{r}}-v_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}+|v_{i}-\ell_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}+\frac{\ell_{k_{r}}-v_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}+|v_{i}-\ell_{i}|\leq\frac{p_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}+\frac{\kappa}{e(k_{r},i)}+\kappa.

It follows that
p_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}>0
from our assumption that
|p_{i}-\ell_{i}|
is large. Thus
k_{r}\in\mathcal{S}^{+}
, completing the proof.

Proof of
(2)
.

Suppose
\ell_{i}>p_{i}
and
i\notin D_{T}
. In this case
\ell_{i}\in B_{q}
for some
1\leq q\leq\sigma
but
\ell_{i}\neq m_{q}
. Then
\ell_{i}=\frac{\ell_{m_{q}}}{e(m_{q},i)}
, and since
u_{m_{q}}\leq e(k_{q},i)u_{i}
we have

\displaystyle 0<\ell_{i}-p_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{m_{q}}}{e(m_{q},i)}-u_{i}-(p_{i}-u_{i})\leq\frac{\ell_{m_{q}}}{e(m_{q},i)}-\frac{u_{m_{q}}}{e(m_{q},i)}-(p_{i}-u_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{m_{q}}-p_{m_{q}}}{e(m_{q},i)}-\frac{(u_{m_{q}}-p_{m_{q}})}{e(m_{q},i)}-(p_{i}-u_{i})

Again it follows that
\ell_{m_{q}}-p_{m_{q}}>0
and so
m_{q}\in\mathcal{T}^{+}
. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

We will also need to introduce other Calderón-type reproducing formulas, with sums restricted to “large” elements of
\mathcal{L}
. Fix
L=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n})\in\mathcal{L}
and let
\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}=\big\{L^{\prime}=(\ell_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\ell_{n}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{L}:\,\ell_{i}^{\prime}\geq\ell_{i}\big\}
. If
L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}
let
A_{L,L^{\prime}}=\big\{i:\ell_{i}^{\prime}>\ell_{i}\big\}
, and define

\displaystyle\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}&\text{if $i\in A(L,L^{\prime})\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\\
\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}&\text{if $i\notin A(L,L^{\prime})\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle\Psi_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#}

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}&\text{if $i\in A(L,L^{\prime})\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\\
\widetilde{\varphi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}&\text{if $i\notin A(L,L^{\prime})\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#}

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#}.

Note that
\Psi^{\#}_{L,L^{\prime}}
has cancellation in the variable
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}^{\#}_{L,L^{\prime}}
has cancellation of order
m
in the variable
\mathbf{x}_{i}
only when
i\in D_{L^{\prime}}\cap A_{L,L^{\prime}}
. In particular,
\Psi^{\#}_{L,L}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}^{\#}_{L,L}
have no cancellation. The following is then a variant of Proposition
4.3
, and we omit the proof.

Lemma 5.4
.

If
L\in\mathcal{L}
and
L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}
then

\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}(\Psi_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}=\delta_{{\mathbf{0}}}.

Proof.

Let

B_{L,L^{\prime}}=\Big\{L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}:\begin{cases}\ell_{i}^{\prime\prime}=\ell_{i}^{\prime}&\text{if $i\in A_{L,L^{\prime}}\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\\
0\leq\ell_{i}^{\prime\prime}\leq\ell_{i}^{\prime}&\text{ if $i\notin A_{L,L^{\prime}}\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\end{cases}\Big\}
,
so
\bigcup_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}B(L,L^{\prime})=\mathbb{N}^{n}
.
Then

(\Psi_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}=\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime\prime}\in B_{L,L^{\prime}}}\mathop{\otimes}\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}(\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime\prime}}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime\prime}})

and so
\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}(\Psi_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}=\mathop{\otimes}_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell_{i}=0}^{\infty}(\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}*\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}})=\delta_{{\mathbf{0}}}
. ∎

For
M>0
let
\chi_{M}(\mathbf{x})=\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)^{-M}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(R^{d})
, let
M>0
, and let
m
be the order of cancellation of
\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}
. There is a constant
\delta>0
depending only on the exponents
\lambda_{i}
and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
, and a constant
C>0
, depending only on
M,m,\delta
so that

\displaystyle\big|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x})\big|\leq C\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-m\delta|L-L^{\prime}|}\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}\big|*\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x})\ ,

Proof.

We have
f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}*(\Psi_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}
from Lemma
5.4
. Now

\displaystyle\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\big(\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#}\big)^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}=\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}=\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}*\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}&\text{if $i\in A(L,L^{\prime})\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\\
\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}=\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}*\varphi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}=\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}*\varphi_{i,\ell^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i})}&\text{if $i\notin A(L,L^{\prime})\cap D_{L^{\prime}}$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\psi_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i}^{\prime})}*(\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{\#})^{(-\ell_{i})},

so

\displaystyle f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}*(\Psi_{L,L}^{\#})^{(-L)}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-L^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}*\left[\Psi_{L,L}^{\#}*(\widetilde{\Psi}_{L,L^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(L-L^{\prime})}\right]^{(-L)}.

Now from Lemma
5.2
, there exists
h_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
, normalized relative to
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
, so that

\displaystyle\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{\#}*(\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell_{i}))}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=\begin{cases}2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell_{i})}h_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $i\in D_{L^{\prime}}\cap A_{L,L^{\prime}}$}\\
h_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})&\text{if $i\notin D_{L^{\prime}}\cap A_{L,L^{\prime}}$}\end{cases}.

(5.4)

To complete the proof we claim that
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{\#}*(\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell_{i}))}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=2^{-\delta m|L^{\prime}-L|}\prod_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
where
\delta>0
depends on the exponents
\{\lambda_{i}\}
and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
. From (
5.4
) we only need additional estimates for factors with
i\notin D_{L^{\prime}}\cap A_{L,L^{\prime}}
. If
i\in D_{L}\setminus A_{L,L^{\prime}}
then
\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell=0
and so
\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}^{\#}*(\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\prime}}^{\#})^{(-(L^{\prime}-L))}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}m(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell_{i})}h_{i}^{(-(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell_{i}))}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
. If
i\notin D_{L^{\prime}}
then
i\in A_{r}
for some
1\leq r\leq s
and
k_{r}\in D_{L^{\prime}}\cap A_{L,L^{\prime}}
. Then
|\ell_{i}-\ell_{i}^{\prime}|=\ell_{i}-\ell_{i}^{\prime}\leq c_{1}|\ell_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}^{\prime}|+c_{2}
where
c_{1},c_{2}
where
c_{1},c_{2}>0
depend only on
\kappa
and the matrix
\mathbf{E}
. Then we can “borrow” part of the estimate
2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{k_{r}}m(\ell_{k_{r}}^{\prime}-\ell_{k_{r}})}
to obtain a gain
2^{-\delta m(\ell_{i}^{\prime}-\ell_{i})}
. This establishes the claim, and completes the proof.
∎

We introduce two auxiliary functions related to the function
f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}
.

\displaystyle\omega_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1+2^{\ell_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}|)^{M}}

(5.5)

\displaystyle=2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}\ell_{i}}\sup\nolimits_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-M|L-L^{\prime}|}\frac{\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\ell_{i}}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{i}|\big)^{M}}

\displaystyle=2^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}\ell_{i}}\sup\nolimits_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-M|L-L^{\prime}|}\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\ .

Obviously
\omega_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})\leq\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, let
M>0
, let
m
be the order of cancellation of
\widetilde{\psi}_{i,\ell_{i}}
, and let
\delta
be the constant from Lemma
5.5
. There is a constant
C>0
depending only on
M,m,\delta
so that

\displaystyle\omega_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})\leq C\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-m\delta|L-L^{\prime}|}\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}\big|*\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}).

Proof.

From Lemma
5.5
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\Big|

\displaystyle\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\leq C\sum_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}\!\!2^{-m\delta|L-L^{\prime}|}\!\!\int\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})d\mathbf{y}.

From the general inequality

\displaystyle\chi_{M}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\leq C\,2^{\sum_{i}q_{i}\ell^{\prime}_{i}}\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{y})

(5.6)

for
L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}
applied with
L^{\prime}=L
, we obtain

\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\int\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\,dy\leq C2^{\sum_{i}q_{i}\ell_{i}}\int\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}\ .

The right-hand side is independent of
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

For
r\leq 1
and
rM>1
, we have the inequality

\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})^{r}\leq C_{M}\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-rM|L-L^{\prime}|}\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}\big|^{r}*\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x})\ .

Proof.

From Lemma
5.5
and equation (
5.6
)

\displaystyle 2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}

\displaystyle\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L^{\prime}}^{+}}2^{-m\delta|L^{\prime\prime}-L^{\prime}|}\Big(\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|*\chi_{M}^{(-L^{\prime})}\Big)(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}2^{\sum_{i}q_{i}\ell_{i}}\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L^{\prime}}^{+}}2^{-m\delta|L^{\prime\prime}-L^{\prime}|}\Big(\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|*\chi_{M}^{(-L)}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\ .

We majorize the convolution as follows:

\displaystyle\Big(\big|

\displaystyle f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|*\chi_{M}^{(-L)}\Big)(\mathbf{x})=\int\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sup_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\Big)^{1-r}\int\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|^{r}\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})^{r}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(2^{\sum_{i}q_{i}\ell_{i}}2^{M|L^{\prime\prime}-L|}\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})\Big)^{1-r}\int\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|^{r}\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})^{r}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2^{(1-r)M|L^{\prime\prime}-L|}\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})^{1-r}\Big(\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|^{r}*\chi_{rM}^{(-L)}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\ ,

where we have used the identity
\big(\chi_{M}^{(-L)}\big)^{r}=2^{-(1-r)\sum_{i}q_{i}\ell_{i}}\chi_{rM}^{(-L)}
. Putting this into the previous formula we obtain

\displaystyle 2^{-\sum_{i}q_{i}\ell_{i}}

\displaystyle 2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\chi_{M}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})^{1-r}\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L^{\prime}}^{+}}2^{-\mu|L^{\prime\prime}-L^{\prime}|}2^{(1-r)M|L^{\prime\prime}-L|}\Big(\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|^{r}*\chi_{rM}^{(-L)}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\ .

Taking the supremum over
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}
, the left-hand side gives
\omega^{*}_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})
. The right-hand side does not depend on
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
can be further majorized by exchanging the order of the supremum in
L^{\prime}
and the sum to give

\displaystyle\omega^{*}_{ML}(\mathbf{x})^{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{(1-r)M|L^{\prime\prime}-L|}\Big(\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|^{r}*\chi_{rM}^{(-L)}\Big)(\mathbf{x})\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}\\
L\leq L^{\prime}\leq L^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}}\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}2^{-M|L-L^{\prime}|}2^{-\mu|L^{\prime\prime}-L^{\prime}|}\ .

Since

2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}2^{-\mu|L^{\prime\prime}-L^{\prime}|}=2^{-M(|L^{\prime}|-|L|)}2^{-\mu(|L^{\prime\prime}|-|L^{\prime}|)}=2^{M|L|}2^{-\mu|L^{\prime\prime}|}2^{(\mu-M)|L^{\prime}|}
, taking
m
sufficiently large, the sup is reached at
L^{\prime}=L^{\prime\prime}
, so that

\displaystyle\omega^{*}_{M,L}(x)^{r}\lesssim\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-rM|L^{\prime\prime}-L|}\Big(\big|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime\prime})}\big|^{r}*\chi_{rM}^{(-L)}\Big)(\mathbf{x}).\qquad\qed

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

To estimate of
S_{\Psi^{\prime}}f(\mathbf{x})=\big[\sum_{P\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\big(\Psi_{P}^{\prime}\big)^{(-P)}\big|^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}
, the starting point is the identity
f*(\Psi_{P}^{\prime})^{(-P)}=\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Big(f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}\Big)*\Big((\Psi_{P}^{\prime})^{(-P)}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}\Big)
from Proposition
4.3
. Then

\displaystyle\big|f*{\Psi_{P}^{\prime}}^{(-P)}(\mathbf{x})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\,\,\big|{\Psi_{P}^{\prime}}^{(-P)}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\omega_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})\int\big|{\Psi_{P}^{\prime}}^{(-P)}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\big|\chi_{M}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}2^{-\delta m|P-L|}\omega_{M,L}(\mathbf{x}).

Using Young’s inequality on
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
it follows that

\displaystyle\Big(\sum\nolimits_{P\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*{\Psi^{\prime}_{P}}^{(-P)}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}}2^{-\varepsilon|L-L^{\prime}|}\omega_{M,L^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})\Big)^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.8)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\omega_{M,L}^{2}(\mathbf{x})\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

Thus it suffices to show that
\big\|\big(\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\omega_{M,L}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{1}\leq C\|S_{\Psi}f\|_{1}
. From Lemma
5.7
, we obtain, for every
M>1/r
, the inequality

\omega_{M,L}(\mathbf{x})^{r}\leq C_{M}\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}\mathcal{M}_{s}\big(|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}|^{r}\big)(\mathbf{x})\ ,

(5.9)

where
\mathcal{M}_{s}
is the strong maximal operator

\mathcal{M}_{s}f(\mathbf{x})=\sup\nolimits_{0<r_{i}\leq 1}\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}r_{i}^{-q_{i}}\int_{|\mathbf{y}_{i}|\leq r_{i}}\big|f(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big|\,d\mathbf{y}\Big)\ .

(5.10)

Going back to (
5.8
), it follows from (
5.9
) and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\omega_{M,L}^{2}(x)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M}\bigg(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{L}^{+}}2^{-M|L^{\prime}-L|}\mathcal{M}_{s}\big(|f*\Psi_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}|^{r}\big)\Big)^{\frac{2}{r}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big(\mathcal{M}_{s}(|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}|^{r})\big)^{\frac{2}{r}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

Finally,

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\omega_{M,L}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M}\big\|\big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{M}_{s}\big(|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}|^{r}\big)^{\frac{2}{r}}\big)^{\frac{r}{2}}\big\|_{1/r}^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{M}\big\|\big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}|^{2}\big)^{\frac{r}{2}}\big\|_{1/r}^{1/r}=C^{\prime}_{M}\big\|\big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{1}\ ,

by the vector-valued strong maximal theorem. This completes the proof .
∎

Theorem
5.1
establishes the equivalence of
S_{\Psi}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
S_{\Psi^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if the families
\Psi
and
\Psi^{\prime}
are constructed via tensor products of reproducing formulas on each
\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
. It is useful to observe that the single inequality (
5.1
) also holds with weaker conditions imposed on
\Psi^{\prime}
. Thus suppose
\Theta=\big\{\Theta_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a family uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm satisfying the cancellation conditions

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}\Theta_{L}(\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{i},\ldots)\,d\mathbf{x}_{i}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\text{for all $i\in D_{L}$}.

(5.11)

Note that
\Theta_{L}
is
not
required to be a tensor product. Let
S_{\Theta}f=\big(\sum\nolimits_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Theta_{L}^{(-L)}\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Without further assumptions on
\Theta
we have the following one-sided inequality.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\Psi=\big\{\Psi_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
be a family given by tensor products as in Theorem
5.1
. There is a constant
C>0
depending only on the Schwartz norms of
\Theta
so that if
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and if
S_{\Psi}f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then
S_{\Theta}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\|S_{\Theta}f\|_{L^{1}}\leq C\|S_{\Psi}f\|_{L^{1}}
.

The proof is contained in that of Theorem
5.1
, once the following reduction to tensor products is established.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
F\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, let
D\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and suppose
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}F(\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{i},\ldots)\,d\mathbf{x}_{i}=0
for
i\in D
. For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
there are functions
f_{i,m}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
,
1\leq i\leq n
, such that

(1)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}}f_{i,m}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\,d\mathbf{x}_{i}=0
if
i\in D
;

(2)

the Schwartz norms of
f_{i,m}
decay rapidly in
m
; precisely, for every
N,M\in\mathbb{N}
there exist
N^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and
C_{M,N}>0
such that
\|f_{i,m}\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N,M}(1+m)^{-M}\|F\|_{(N^{\prime})}^{1/n}
;

(3)

F(\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n})=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{i,m}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)
.

Proof.

Begin by ignoring the cancellations imposed on
F
. Let
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be supported on the cube
Q=[-1,1]^{d}
and satisfy
\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\chi(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{k})\equiv 1
. Then
F_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{x})=F(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{k})\chi(\mathbf{x})
is supported in
Q
, and
\|F_{\mathbf{k}}\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N,M}\big(1+|\mathbf{k}|\big)^{-M}\|F\|_{(N\vee M)}
for every
N,M
. Let
F_{\mathbf{k}}^{\rm per}
denote the periodic extension of
F_{\mathbf{k}}
(restricted to
2Q
) having period 4 in each variable.
F_{\mathbf{k}}^{\rm per}
has a Fourier expansion

F_{\mathbf{k}}^{\rm per}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}a_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}h_{j}x_{j}}
. Choose
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
supported on
[-2,2]
and equal to 1 on
[-1,1]
so that
\eta^{\#}(\mathbf{x})=\prod_{j=1}^{d}\eta(x_{j})
is supported on
2Q
and equal to 1 on
Q
. Set
\eta^{\#}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=\prod_{j\in E_{i}}\eta(x_{j})
and
\mathbf{h}_{i}=(h_{j})_{j\in E_{i}}
. Putting
g_{i,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=a_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}^{1/n}\eta^{\#}(\mathbf{x}_{i})e^{i\pi\mathbf{h}_{i}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i}}
we have

\displaystyle F_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{x})\eta^{\#}(\mathbf{x})=F_{\mathbf{k}}^{\rm per}(\mathbf{x})\eta^{\#}(\mathbf{x})=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}a_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\big(\eta^{\#}(\mathbf{x}_{i})e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\sum\nolimits_{j\in E_{i}}h_{j}x_{j}}\big)=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}g_{i,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),

and so
F(\mathbf{x})=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}F_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{k})=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\prod\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}g_{i,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{k}_{i})
. For every
P\in\mathbb{N}
, the coefficients
a_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}
satisfy the estimate
\big|a_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}\big|\leq C^{\prime}_{P}\big(1+|\mathbf{h}|)^{-P}\|F_{\mathbf{k}}\|_{(P)}\leq C^{\prime}_{M,P}\big(1+|\mathbf{h}|)^{-P}\big(1+|\mathbf{k}|\big)^{-M}\|F\|_{(P\vee M)}
. Thus
\big\|g_{i,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}\big\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\big(1+|\mathbf{k}|\big)^{N}\|g_{i,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}}\leq C_{N,P,M}|\mathbf{h}|^{N}\big(1+|\mathbf{h}|)^{-\frac{P}{n}}\big(1+|\mathbf{k}|\big)^{-\frac{M}{n}}\|F\|_{(P\vee M)}^{\frac{1}{n}}
for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
. This gives the estimates
\|f_{i,m}\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N,M}(m+1)^{-M}\|F\|_{(N^{\prime})}^{1/n}
up to well-ordering the pairs
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{h})
by increasing modulus and choosing
P
appropriately.

Now suppose that
F
satisfies the cancellation conditions. It follows from Lemma
4.1
that
F=\sum\nolimits_{q\in\prod_{i=1}^{n}E_{i}}\partial_{y_{q_{1}}}\cdots\partial_{y_{q_{n}}}F_{q}
with
F_{q}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\|F_{q}\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\|F\|_{(N)}
for every
q
. It is then sufficient to apply the previous result to
F_{q}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.8
.

Given
\Theta=\big\{\Theta_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
satisfying (
5.11
), write
\Theta_{L}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\Theta_{m,L}
where
\Theta_{m,L}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{n})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i,m,L}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
is a tensor product. Then by Minkowski’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\Theta}f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\Big[\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}|f*\Theta_{L}(\mathbf{x})|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\Big[\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Big|\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}f*\Psi_{m,L}(\mathbf{x})\Big|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\Big|\Big|\Big\{\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}f*\Psi_{m,L}(\mathbf{x})\Big\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Big|\Big|_{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{L})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\Big|\Big|\Big\{f*\Psi_{m,L}(\mathbf{x})\Big\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Big|\Big|_{\ell^{2}(\mathcal{L})}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\Psi_{m}}f(\mathbf{x}).

Repeating the proof of Theorem
5.1
, we obtain that
\big|\big|\mathcal{S}_{\Theta_{m}}f\big|\big|_{L^{1}}\leq\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\Theta_{m}\|_{(M)}\big|\big|S_{\Psi}f\big|\big|_{L^{1}}
and the proof is completed applying Lemma
5.9
.
∎

6.
L^{1}
-equivalence of multi-norm square functions of convolution and tensor type

In this section we study the multi-norm square functions of convolution type defined in Section
4.4
. The first step is to consider two families
\Sigma=\big\{\Sigma_{L},{\rm P}_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
and
\Sigma^{\prime}=\big\{\Sigma^{\prime}_{L},{\rm P}^{\prime}_{L},:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
constructed via convolution from
\big\{\rho_{i,\ell}\big\}
and
\big\{\rho^{\prime}_{i,\ell}\big\}
respectively. We first prove that any two square functions of convolution type are
L^{1}
-equivalent and then prove
L^{1}
- equivalence between specific square functions of different types.

Remark 6.1
.

In general, the study of the functions
\Sigma_{L}
cannot be reduced to the study of the functions
\Psi_{L}
appearing in square functions of tensor type because they do not necessarily have cancellation in single coordinates
\mathbf{x}_{i}
. Note however that if we assume that the Fourier transform of the factors
\sigma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}
is supported near the sets
B^{i}_{\ell_{i}}
introduced in Section
2.1
, then the Fourier transform of
\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}
is supported near the annular set
B_{L}
in (
2.6
), and hence

\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}
gains cancellation in each variable
\mathbf{x}_{i}
with
i\in D_{L}
(see the discussion in Section
3.3
and the proof of Proposition
6.5
below).
This indicates that also for general
\sigma_{i,\ell_{i}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with integral zero, the convolution
\mathop{*}_{i\in D_{L}}\sigma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})}
generates some implicit cancellation in the single variables that are not immediately apparent from the formulas.

6.1.
Independence of the choice of
\big\{\Sigma_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}

We show that the condition
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
does not depend on the choice of
\{\Sigma_{L}:L\in\mathcal{L}\}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}
be two families as in Section
4.3
, and let
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}
,
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
be the corresponding square functions (
4.8
). Given
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the conditions
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\in L^{1}
and
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}
are equivalent.

This statement will be a direct consequence of the following analogue of Proposition
5.8
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\Sigma
be the family of functions introduced in Section
4.3
, and for
1\leq i\leq n
and
L\in\mathcal{L}
let
\{\gamma_{i,L}\}
be a family of Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm such that, if
L\in\mathcal{L}_{D}
, then
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\gamma_{i,L}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=0
for all
i\in D
.
Define
\Gamma_{L}
so that
\Gamma_{L}^{(-L)}=\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i,L}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}
.
Then
\big\|\big(\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Gamma_{L}^{(-L)}\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{1}\leq C\big\|S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\big\|_{1}
where
C>0
depending only on the bounds for Schwartz norms of
\gamma_{i,L}
.

With a single addition the proof follows the same lines as that of Theorem
4.7
.

Proof.

The only point that requires some comment is Lemma
5.4
. We prove that required estimate remains valid in the form:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\ell^{\prime}_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)\Big)^{M}\big|\Gamma_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}\leq C2^{-\varepsilon|L-L^{\prime}|}\ ,

uniformly in
L,L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
, provided
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}
has cancellations of sufficiently high order relative to
M
and
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small. For each convolution
\gamma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}*\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}
, we apply Lemma
5.3
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to obtain that, with
m
equal to the order of cancellation of
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}
,

\gamma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}*\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}=\begin{cases}2^{-m\bar{\lambda}_{i}(\ell^{\prime}_{i}-\ell_{i})}h_{i,L,L^{\prime}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}&\text{ if }\ell_{i}\leq\ell^{\prime}_{i}\\
2^{-\bar{\lambda}_{i}(\ell_{i}-\ell^{\prime}_{i})}h_{i,L,L^{\prime}}^{(-\ell^{\prime}_{i})_{i}}&\text{ if }\ell^{\prime}_{i}<\ell_{i}\ ,\end{cases}

(6.1)

where
h_{i,L,L^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with Schwartz norms controlled by those of
\gamma_{i,\ell_{i}}
and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}
.
Hence, with
\bar{\ell}_{i}=\min\{\ell_{i},\ell^{\prime}_{i}\}
and
\mu_{i}(L,L^{\prime})
equal to the exponent of 2 in (
6.1
),

\Gamma_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}=2^{-\sum_{i}\mu_{i}(L,L^{\prime})}\Big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}h_{i,L,L^{\prime}}^{(-\bar{\ell}_{i})_{i}}\Big)\ .

We apply Lemma
4.5
and Lemma
3.9
(iii) to obtain that the functions
k_{L,L^{\prime}}=\big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}h_{i,L,L^{\prime}}^{(-\widetilde{\ell}_{i})_{i}}\big)^{(\widetilde{L})}
, with
\widetilde{L}\in\mathcal{L}
closest to
\bar{L}=L\wedge L^{\prime}
, are uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm. We obtain, with
N
sufficiently large relative to
M
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\ell^{\prime}_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)\Big)^{M}

\displaystyle\big|\Gamma_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}^{(-L^{\prime})}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=2^{-\sum_{i}\mu_{i}(L,L^{\prime})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\ell^{\prime}_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)\Big)^{M}\big|k_{L,L^{\prime}}^{(-\widetilde{L})}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq C_{N}2^{\sum_{i}(q_{i}\widetilde{\ell}_{i}-\mu_{i}(L,L^{\prime}))}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\ell^{\prime}_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)\big)^{M}}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+\widehat{N}_{i}(2^{\widetilde{\ell}_{1}}\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,2^{\widetilde{\ell}_{n}}\mathbf{x}_{n})\big)^{-N}}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{N}2^{-\sum_{i}(q_{i}\widetilde{\ell}_{i}-\mu_{i}(L,L^{\prime}))}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\ell^{\prime}_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)\big)^{M}}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big(1+2^{\widetilde{\ell}_{i}}|\mathbf{x}_{i}|\big)^{-N^{\prime}}}\,dx\ ,

where (
4.5
) is used in last inequality and the
C_{N}
subsumes the norms in
\|h\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{(N^{\prime})}
of the single factors. We then repeat the proof of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

6.2.
L^{1}
-equivalence with the square functions of tensor type

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
\Psi=\{\Psi_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}
be a family as in (
4.4
) of Section
4.2
constructed via tensor products, and let
\Sigma=\{\Sigma_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}
be a family as in Section
4.3
constructed via convolution. Let
S_{\Psi}
,
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}
be the corresponding square functions (
4.7
) and (
4.8
). Then, given
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the two conditions
S_{\Psi}f\in L^{1}
and
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\in L^{1}
are equivalent.

Having Theorem
6.2
at our disposal, it suffices to prove that, for appropriately chosen pairs of families
(\Psi,\Sigma)
and
(\Psi^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
we have the implication
S_{\Psi}f\in L^{1}\Longrightarrow S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\in L^{1}
and the implication
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}\Longrightarrow S_{\Psi^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\big\{\sigma_{i,L}:i=1,\dots,n\,,\,L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}\subset\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm and assume that, if
L\in\mathcal{L}_{D}
with
D\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
, then

\displaystyle\text{\rm supp\,}\widehat{\sigma_{i,L}}\subset\begin{cases}\big\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}:2^{-3/4}\leq\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\leq 2^{3/4}\big\}&\text{ if }i\in D\\
\big\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}:\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\leq 2^{3/4}\big\}&\text{ if }i\not\in D\end{cases}.

If
\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}=\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i,L}^{(-\ell_{i})}
then
\big\|\big(\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\big|f*\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}\big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{1}\leq C\big\|S_{\Psi}f\big\|_{1}
, with
C
depending on the bounds for the
\sigma_{i,L}
in an appropriate Schwartz norm, and this establishes the implication
S_{\Psi}f\in L^{1}\Longrightarrow S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\in L^{1}
.

Proof.

We prove that the functions
\Sigma_{L}
are uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfy the cancellation conditions in (
5.11
). In fact Lemma
4.5
gives uniform bounds on Schwartz norms of the
\Sigma_{L}
. In order to verify the cancellation conditions, we need a detailed description of the support of
\mathcal{F}[\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}]=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{F}[\sigma_{i,L}^{(-\ell_{i})}]
.
We have

\displaystyle\text{\rm supp\,}\widehat{\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}}

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcap_{i\in D}\big\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}:2^{-\ell_{i}-3/4}\leq\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\leq 2^{-\ell_{i}+3/4}\big\}\cap\bigcap_{i\not\in D}\big\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}:\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\leq 2^{-\ell_{i}+3/4}\big\}

\displaystyle\subset\big\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}:2^{\ell_{i}-3/4}\leq|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq 2^{\ell_{i}+3/4}\text{ if }i\in D\,,\,|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq 2^{\ell_{i}+3/4}\text{ if }i\not\in D\big\}.

Since
\ell_{i}\geq 1
for
i\in D
by (
3.13
), the support of
\widehat{\Sigma_{L}^{(-L)}}
does not intersect the coordinate subspaces
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}={\mathbf{0}}
for
i\in D
, and this gives the required cancellations.
∎

We now turn to the second implication. For each
i=1,\dots,n
, fix functions
\varphi_{i,\ell}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
, so that
\widehat{\varphi_{i,\ell}}
is 1 on the set
\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}:|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\}
and is supported in
\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}:|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq 2\}
. Then, for
\ell>0
, the functions
\psi_{i,\ell}
in (
4.1
) have Fourier transforms supported in the set
\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}:1/4\leq|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq 2\}
.
From these functions we construct
\Psi_{L}
, for
L\in\mathcal{L}
according to (
4.4
).

Lemma 6.6
.

For every
L\in\mathcal{L}
, there exists a family
\big\{\gamma_{i,L}:i=1,\dots,n\,,\,L\in\mathcal{L}\big\}
satisfying the hypothesis of Proposition
6.3
and such that

\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}=\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i,L}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}.

Proof.

The Fourier transform of
\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}
is supported, for
L\in\mathcal{L}_{D}
, on the set

E_{L}=\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\in D}\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}:2^{\ell_{i}-2}\leq|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq 2^{\ell_{i}+1}\}\Big)\times\Big(\prod\nolimits_{i\not\in D}\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}:|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\leq 2^{\ell_{i}+1}\}\Big)\ .

We claim that
E_{L}\subset\big\{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}:A2^{\ell_{i}}\leq\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\leq B2^{\ell_{i}}\big\}
for appropriate constants
B>A>0
and
i=1,\dots,n
. In fact, for
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in E_{L}
, Lemma
3.9
(i) gives the upper bound
\widehat{N}_{i}(\xi)=\max_{j}|\xi_{j}|^{\frac{1}{e(j,i)}}\leq c\max_{j}2^{\frac{\ell_{j}}{e(j,i)}}\leq c2^{\ell_{i}+C\kappa}
. The lower bound is obvious if
i\in D
, because

\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\geq|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}|\geq 2^{\ell_{i}-2}
. If
i\not\in D
, we use Lemma
3.9
(i) again and take
\mathbf{t}\in F(D)
at distance at most
\kappa
from
L
. Then there is
k\in D
such that
t_{k}=e(k,i)t_{i}
. Then

\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\geq|{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{k}|^{\frac{1}{e(k,i)}}\geq 2^{\frac{\ell_{k}-2}{e(k,i)}}\geq 2^{\frac{t_{k}-\kappa-2}{e(k,i)}}\geq 2^{t_{i}-\frac{\kappa+2}{e(k,i)}}\geq 2^{\ell_{i}-C^{\prime}}.

For
i=1,\dots,n
, fix a smooth function
\eta_{i}
, equal to 1 on the set
\big\{\xi:A\leq\widehat{N}_{i}(\xi)\leq B\big\}
and supported in
\big\{\xi:A/2\leq\widehat{N}_{i}(\xi)\leq 2B\big\}
. Then define functions
\gamma_{i,L}
by
\widehat{\gamma_{i,L}}=\widehat{\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}}\eta_{i}
. Recalling that
\widehat{\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}}
only depends on the variable
\xi_{i}
, it follows that
\widehat{\gamma_{i,L}}(2^{-\ell_{i}}\cdot_{i}\xi)=\widehat{\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}}(2^{-\ell_{i}}\xi_{i})\eta_{i}(2^{-\ell_{i}}\cdot_{i}\xi)
, and therefore

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\gamma_{i,L}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\psi_{i,\ell_{i}}}(2^{-\ell_{i}}\xi_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}(2^{-\ell_{i}}\cdot_{i}\xi)\Big)=\widehat{\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}}(\xi)\ .

Notice that
\text{\rm supp\,}\gamma_{i,L}
contains
{\mathbf{0}}
only if
L=0
, so that the cancellations required by Proposition
6.3
are satisfied.
∎

It now follows from Proposition
6.3
that if
\big\{\Psi_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}
as in Lemma
6.6
and if
\big\{\Sigma_{L}\}_{L\in\mathcal{L}}
as in in Section
4.3
then
\big\|S_{\Psi}f\big\|_{1}\leq C\big\|S^{\prime}_{\Sigma}f\big\|_{1}
. This gives the second implication
S^{\prime}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}\Longrightarrow S_{\Psi^{\prime}}f\in L^{1}
, and this completes the proof of Theorem
6.4
.

6.3.
Square functions of convolution type with parameters varying in
\mathbb{N}^{n}

Keeping the same kind of family
\big\{\sigma_{i,\ell}:i=1,\dots,n\,,\,\ell\in\mathbb{N}\big\}\subset\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
appearing in (
4.6
), we define the following square function:

S^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}f=\Big(\sum_{L\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}\big|f*\big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i,\ell_{i}}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\big)\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(6.2)

In this section we first prove independence of the condition
S^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
from the choice of the family
\big\{\sigma_{i,\ell}\big\}
, and then its equivalence with the same condition on the previously considered square functions.
In analogy with the arguments for the previously considered square functions, we prove the following.

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
\{\eta_{i,L}\}_{i=1,\dots,n\,,\,L\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}
be a family of functions in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm and such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\eta_{i,L}(x)\,dx=0\ .

for all pairs
(i,L)
for which
\ell_{i}>0
.
Then

\Big\|\Big(\sum_{L\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}\big|f*\big(\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i,L}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}\big)\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{1}\leq C\big\|S^{\prime\prime}_{\sigma}f\big\|_{1}\ ,

(6.3)

with
C
depending on the uniform bound on the
\eta_{i,L}
in an appropriate Schwartz norm.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
, given
L\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
, we can express any convolution
\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}
as
h_{L}^{(-\mathbf{t}_{L})}
with
\mathbf{t}_{L}\in\Gamma({\mathbf{E}})
and
h_{L}
satisfying the inequalities of Lemma
4.5
. By Lemma
3.9
(ii), there is a map
\mathbb{N}^{n}\ni L\longrightarrow\widetilde{L}\in\mathcal{L}
so that
\widetilde{L}
is at uniformly bounded distance from
\mathbf{t}_{L}
and
\mathop{*}_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}^{(-\ell_{i})_{i}}=k_{L}^{(-\widetilde{L})}
with
\|k_{L}\|_{(N)}\leq C_{N}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{(N^{\prime})}
. Applying this procedure with
f_{i}
replaced by
\eta_{i,\ell_{i}}
, respectively
\sigma_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}},\widetilde{\sigma}_{i,\ell^{\prime}_{i}}
, we denote by
\eta^{*}_{L}
, respectively
\sigma^{*}_{L^{\prime}},\widetilde{\sigma}^{*}_{L^{\prime}}
