\displaystyle=\sup_{\|h\|_{2}\leq 1}\Big|\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})}\int_{{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}\big[\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}\big]^{\vee}*h(\mathbf{y})\,\,\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})}|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f|^{2}{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{2}\sup_{\|h\|_{2}\leq 1}\Big\|\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})}|\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*h|^{2}{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{i,\mathbf{R}}\big|\mathbf{R}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\|h\|_{2}\leq 1}\Big\|\Big(\sum_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{L}_{S}}|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*h|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{2}\leq C\,\lambda_{i,\mathbf{R}}\big|\mathbf{R}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

To complete the case
s=1
it remains to show that
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\sharp}}|\lambda_{i,\mathbf{R}}|\leq C\|f\|_{\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{E}}^{1}}
.
We use the inequality
\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\sharp}}|\mathbf{R}|\leq|\Omega^{i}_{s}|
, due to the fact that maximal rectangles are essentially disjoint. From this, Lemma
9.4
, the fact that
\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f\big|_{\rm PP}
is constant, and the
L^{2}
-boundedness of the maximal operator, we obtain, for every
i
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\sharp}}|\lambda_{i,\mathbf{R}}|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\sharp}}|\mathbf{R}|\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\,\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}\,d\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(9.7)

\displaystyle\leq|\Omega^{i}_{s}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\,\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f\big|_{\rm PP}^{2}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2|\Omega^{i}_{s}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\mathbf{M}_{S}[{\mathbf{1}}_{\Omega^{i}_{s}\setminus\Omega^{i+1}}](\mathbf{x})\,\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f\big|_{\rm PP}^{2}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2|\Omega^{i}_{s}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\Big(\mathbf{M}_{S}\big[{\mathbf{1}}_{\Omega^{i}_{s}\setminus\Omega^{i+1}}\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f\big|_{\rm PP}\big](\mathbf{x})\Big)^{2}\,d\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\Omega^{i}_{s}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{\Omega^{i}_{s}\setminus\Omega^{i+1}}\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f\big|_{\rm PP}^{2}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

For every
L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
, almost every
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is contained in at most one rectangle
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{L}
, so that
\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\big|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f\big|_{\rm PP}^{2}(\mathbf{x})\leq(S_{\Psi}^{\rm PP}f)^{2}(\mathbf{x})
. Since
S_{\Psi}^{\rm PP}f(\mathbf{x})\leq 2^{i+1}
for
\mathbf{x}\not\in\Omega^{i+1}
, we conclude that

|\lambda_{i,\mathbf{R}}|\leq C|\Omega^{i}_{s}|2^{i}
.
Summing over
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the conclusion in the case
s=1
follows from (
9.2
).

Case 3:
s\geq 2
.
Given a marked partition
S
with
s\geq 2
, we will write
f_{S}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{i,S}\,a_{i,s}
where the atom
a_{i,S}
is associated to the set
\Omega_{s}^{i}
and can be decomposed as in Definition
9.1
(
3
). To do this we modify the definitions in (
9.3
). Again let
\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}=\big\{\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}:\text{$|\mathbf{R}\cap\Omega^{i}|>\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{R}|$ and $|\mathbf{R}\cap\Omega^{i+1}|\leq\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{R}|$}\big\}
, but instead of using
\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i,\sharp}
(the maximal rectangles in
\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}
), we use
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{S}^{i,\sharp}=\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})
, the maximal rectangles in
\Omega_{s}^{i}
, ordered by inclusion. A rectangle of
\ \mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}
may be contained in more than one element of
\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})
, but we can partition
\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}
into disjoint subsets
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})
indexed by rectangles
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})
, so that
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})\Longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{R}
. Then the decomposition corresponding to (
9.4
) is

\displaystyle f_{S}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}.

(9.8)

Let

\lambda_{i,S}=|\Omega^{i}_{s}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\Big\|\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f|^{2}{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{2},

(9.9)

a_{i,S}(\mathbf{x})=\lambda_{i,S}^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}\ .

(9.10)

so that, by (
9.8
),

\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}}\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}*\widetilde{\Psi}_{L}^{(-L)}*f(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\lambda_{i,S}\,a_{i,S}(\mathbf{x})
.
We prove that each
a_{i,S}
is a
(S,\tau)
-atom associated to
\Omega^{i}_{s}
. Define

a_{i,S,\mathbf{R}}=\lambda_{i,S}^{-1}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{S}^{i,\flat}(\mathbf{R})}\int_{{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}

(9.11)

so that
a_{i,S}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})}a_{i,S,\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{x})
.
We prove that the
a_{i,S,\mathbf{R}}
satisfy the requirements for being pre-atoms of
a_{i,S}
.
Condition (
3a
) in Definition
9.1
on the support follows from the formula in (
9.9
) and the cancellation condition (
3b
) is inherited from the factors
\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}
, which have cancellation in the dotted variables in
S
because
L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
. To prove property (
3c
) we use a duality argument as in (
9.6
). Given a partition
\{\mathcal{D}_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of
\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{i,S}\Big\|\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}a_{i,S,\mathbf{R}}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|h\|_{2}\leq 1}\Big|\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{i}_{S}(\mathbf{R})}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\big[\widetilde{\Psi}_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}\big]^{\vee}*h(\mathbf{y})\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}\Big|

(9.12)

\displaystyle\leq C\,\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{i}_{S}(\mathbf{R})}|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{x})|^{2}\,d\mathbf{x}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\ ,

and so

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in J}\Big\|\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}a_{i,S,\mathbf{R}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\lambda_{i,S}^{-2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}^{\sharp}(\Omega_{s}^{i})}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{i}_{S}(\mathbf{R})}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})|^{2}d\mathbf{y}

(9.13)

\displaystyle=C^{2}\lambda_{i,S}^{-2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}^{i}_{S}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}|\Psi_{L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{y})|^{2}d\mathbf{y}\leq C^{2}\big|\Omega^{i}\big|^{-1}\ .

Thus each
a_{i,S,\mathbf{R}}
satisfies (
3c
). To finish the proof of the theorem in Case 3 we must show that
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}|\lambda_{i,S}|\leq C\|S_{\Psi}^{{\rm PP}}f\|_{L^{1}}
. Starting from the formula

\lambda_{i,S}^{2}=|\Omega^{i}_{s}|\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{S}^{i}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}|\Psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}^{(-L_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})}*f(\mathbf{x})|^{2}\,d\mathbf{x}

and repeating the same steps in (
9.7
) we deduce that
\lambda_{i,S}\leq C2^{i}|\Omega^{i}_{s}|

and derive the conclusion summing over
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

10.
A Journé-type covering lemma

In this section we obtain geometric results related to Journé’s covering lemma. This was first stated in
[
24
]
and then further developed by Pipher
[
32
]
, Carbery and Seeger
[
2
]
, and Cabrelli
et.al.

[
1
]
. The content of this section is motivated by these previous works and, in particular, our results are essentially equivalent to those in the geometric part of
[
2
]
. The natural setting for these results is a general product structure and for this reason we temporarily abandon our multi-norm setting. The results we obtain will be used in Section
12
where the relevant product structures on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
will depending on a marked partition
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}
, with factors
\mathbb{R}^{A_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{A_{s}}
and dilations
\delta^{S}_{t}
defined in (
7.5
). In principle we could also remove the locality assumption, but this is not so relevant and we prefer to focus on the local setting.

To establish the basic terminology in a general product structure, we denote the ambient space by
\mathbb{R}^{\nu}=\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{s}}
and by
\{\delta_{t}\}_{t>0}
a family of dilations leaving each subspace
\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{r}}
invariant. Following the definitions in Section
7
, we denote by
\mathbf{Q}^{\ell_{r}}_{r}
, or simply
\mathbf{Q}_{r}
, a dyadic cube in
\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{r}}
and by
\mathbf{R}^{L}=\prod_{r=1}^{s}\mathbf{Q}^{\ell_{r}}_{r}
or simply
\mathbf{R}=\prod_{r=1}^{s}\mathbf{Q}_{r}
, a dyadic rectangle in
\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
. For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
we denote by
2^{\mathbf{m}}\mathbf{R}
the
2^{\mathbf{m}}
dyadic enlargement of
\mathbf{R}
as defined in Section
7.4.1
.

10.1.
Enlargements and embeddedness factors

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
be a set of finite measure. The
standard enlargements

\big\{\Omega^{(j)}:j\geq 0\big\}
of
\Omega
are defined inductively. For
j=0
set
\Omega^{(0)}=\Omega
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{s}
denote the dyadic strong maximal function. Then for
j\geq 1
set

\Omega^{(j)}=\big\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\boldsymbol{\nu}}:\mathcal{M}_{s}[{\mathbf{1}}_{\Omega^{(j-1)}}](\mathbf{x})>1/2\big\}.

(10.1)

It follows from the
L^{2}
-boundedness of
\mathcal{M}_{s}
that there is a constant
C
so that
\big|\Omega^{(j)}\big|\leq C^{j}\big|\Omega\big|
.

Next let
\mathbb{D}(\Omega)
be the family of all dyadic rectangles contained in
\Omega
. Let
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}(\Omega)
and write
2^{m\,\mathbf{e}_{r}}\mathbf{R}=2^{m}\mathbf{Q}_{r}\times\big(\prod_{r^{\prime}\neq r}\mathbf{Q}_{r^{\prime}}\big)
as in Section
7.4.1
. The
embeddedness factor of
\mathbf{R}
in
\Omega
in the variable
\mathbf{x}_{r}
is

\displaystyle{\rm Emb}_{\Omega}^{r}

\displaystyle=2^{\mu}

\displaystyle\text{ where }

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=\max_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\Big\{m:\big|2^{m\,\mathbf{e}_{r}}\mathbf{R}\cap\Omega\big|>\frac{1}{2}\big|2^{m\,\mathbf{e}_{r}}\mathbf{R}\big|\Big\}.

(10.2)

Note that if
\mathbf{R}\subseteq\Omega^{(j)}
and
{\rm Emb}_{\Omega^{(j)}}^{r}(\mathbf{R})=2^{\mu}
then
2^{\mu\mathbf{e}_{r}}\mathbf{R}\subseteq\Omega^{(j+1)}
.

We now introduce enlargements of dyadic rectangles. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{N})\in\big\{1,\ldots,s\big\}^{N}
so that each
r_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,s\}
. (We call
\mathbf{r}
an
N
-string
.) Given
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}(\Omega)
and
0\leq j\leq N
we construct enlargements
\mathbf{R}^{(j)}
of
\mathbf{R}
and multi-indices
\mathbf{m}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})=\big(m_{1}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R}),\dots,m_{s}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})\big)\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
. This is done by induction. For
j=0
set
\mathbf{R}^{(0)}=\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{m}^{(0)}(\mathbf{R})=(0,\ldots,0)
. If
\mathbf{R}^{(j-1)}
and
m^{(j-1)}(\mathbf{R})
have been defined, let
2^{\mu_{j}(\mathbf{R})}={\rm Emb}^{r_{j}}_{\Omega^{(j)}}(\mathbf{R}^{(j-1)})
and put

\displaystyle m^{(j)}_{r_{j}}(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}m^{(j-1)}_{r_{j}}(\mathbf{R})+\mu_{j}(\mathbf{R})&\text{if $r_{j}\in\mathbf{r}$}\\
m^{(j)}_{r^{\prime}}=m^{(j-1)}_{r^{\prime}}&\text{if $r^{\prime}\neq r_{j}$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{(j)}

\displaystyle=2^{\mathbf{m}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})}\mathbf{R}=2^{\mu_{j}(\mathbf{R})\mathbf{e}_{r_{j}}}\mathbf{R}^{(j-1)}.

(10.3)

10.2.
Subfamilies of
\mathbb{D}(\Omega)
and factorizations of
\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
depending on
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}

Let
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(\Omega)
be a family of dyadic rectangles and let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{N})
be an
N
-string. Then
\mathbf{R}^{(j)}
and
\mathbf{m}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})
are defined in (
10.3
) for
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}
and
0\leq j\leq N
. Let
B
be a non-empty subset of
\{1,\ldots,s\}
with cardinality
|B|=b
.

We define certain subfamilies of
\mathcal{U}
depending on
B
and
\mathbf{r}
. To do this we write
\mathbb{R}^{\nu}=\mathbb{R}^{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}=\big(\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{r}}\big)\times\big(\prod\nolimits_{r\notin B}\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{r}}\big)
.
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}
will denote dyadic rectangles in
\mathbb{R}^{B}
and
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
, and if
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}(\Omega)
we write
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbf{R}_{B^{c}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
.
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}
will denote multi-indices in
\mathbb{N}^{B}
and
\mathbb{N}^{B^{c}}
and if
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
we write
\mathbf{m}=(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})
. Given
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}
and
0\leq j\leq N
define

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\big\{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}:\mathbf{m}_{B}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})=\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\big\}

(10.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{B}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\big\{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}:\text{exists }\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\text{ such that }\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\big\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\big\{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}:\mathbf{R}_{B}=\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\big\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\big\{\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}:\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\big\}.

If
|B|=s
only the first two lines of (
10.4
) make sense and define the same object.
With
|B|
-tuples
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}
ordered component-wise, we also define

\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{n}^{\prime}}\quad\text{and similarly for }\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{B}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\ \text{and }\ \overline{\mathcal{V}}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}).

(10.5)

The sets in (
10.4
) and (
10.5
) depend on the choice of an N-string
\mathbf{r}
and the subset
B
.

Definition 10.1
.

(a)

Let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N})\in\{1,\ldots,s\}^{N}
be an
N
-string.
If
B\subseteq\{1,\ldots,s\}
then
\mathbf{r}
contains
B
if there exists a segment of
\mathbf{r}
,
\mathbf{r}_{B}=(r_{k},r_{k+1},\dots,r_{k+b-1})
, which is a list, without repetitions, of all elements of
B
.
\mathbf{r}
is
complete
if it contains every non-empty subset of
\{1,\ldots,s\}
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{A}
is a family of sets, the
shadow
of
\mathcal{A}
is the set
{\rm sh}(\mathcal{A})=\bigcup_{A\in\mathcal{A}}A
.

An example of complete string is the one defined in the proof of Lemma 3.3 of
[
2
]
for general
s
. An earlier example for
s=3
is
\mathbf{r}=(1,2,3,1)
, implictly used in
[
32
]
.

We now have the following proposition, which is essentially contained in
[
2
, Lemmas 2.3, 3.3]
.

Proposition 10.2
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a family of dyadic rectangles contained in
\Omega
,
B\subseteq\{1,\ldots,s\}
a non-empty subset, and
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N})
a string containing
B
. Then there exist
C,p>0
such that, for every
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}
,

\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|\big|{\rm sh}\big(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\big)\big|\leq C\big(1+|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|\big)^{p}|\Omega|.

(10.6)

Proof.

We may assume that
B=\{1,2,\dots,b\}
and

\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{\ell},1,2,\dots,b,r_{\ell+b+1},\dots,r_{N}),

the issue being the validity of (
10.6
) independently on the location of
\mathbf{r}_{B}
inside
\mathbf{r}
.
Lemma 2.3 in
[
2
]
proves (
10.6
) in the case
\mathbf{r}=(1,2,\dots,b)
, and Lemma 3.3 essentially contains the proof in the case where
(1,2,\dots,b)
is the terminal segment of
\mathbf{r}
, that is
N=\ell+b
. It remains to discuss the case where
N>\ell+b
.
But, if
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}
then
\mathbf{m}^{(\ell+b)}(\mathbf{R})\leq\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})
, hence
\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\ell+b,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}
and

\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{\ell+b,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
. Then, applying Lemma 3.3 in
[
2
]
,

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}_{B}\in\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|\big|{\rm sh}\big(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\ell+b,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|\big|{\rm sh}\big(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{\ell+b,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\big)\big|\leq C\big(1+|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|\big)^{p}|\Omega|.\qed

For completeness we add the estimates on the total measure of rectangles enlarged either dyadically or by dilations, as defined in Section
7.4
. Let
\mathcal{U},\Omega,\mathbf{r},N,B
be as above.

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\overline{\Omega}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\mathbf{R}
and
\widehat{\Omega}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\circ\mathbf{R}
.
Then, with appropriate constants independent of
\mathcal{U}
,
|\overline{\Omega}|\sim|\Omega|\sim|\widehat{\Omega}|
.

Proof.

By the choice of
\delta
in (
10.1
) we have
2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\mathbf{R}\subset\Omega^{(N+1)}
. Then the first comparison follows since
|\Omega^{(N+1)}|\leq C^{N+1}|\Omega|
.
By Lemma
7.6
the sets
2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\mathbf{R}
and
2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\circ\mathbf{R}
have comparable measures and this implies that the (ordinary) strong maximal function of
{\mathbf{1}}_{\overline{\Omega}}
is greater than a constant
\gamma>0
on
\widehat{\Omega}
, hence
|\widehat{\Omega}|\leq C|\overline{\Omega}|
.
∎

11.
A convolution estimates for multi-norm kernels

We return to the multi-norm setting with
\mathbf{E}
a standard
n\times n
matrix and
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{n}}
. Let
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathbf{E})
be a multi-norm kernel on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as in Definition
3.10
and let
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfy appropriate support and cancellation conditions. The main result of this section is Proposition
11.1
below which gives estimates for certain integrals of
|f*\mathcal{K}|
.

As usual, if
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{E}}
we write
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{A_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{A_{s}}
. If
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we write
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{x}_{A_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{x}_{A_{s}})
with
\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}=(\mathbf{x}_{i})_{i\in A_{r}}
. If
\mathbf{R}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{i}^{\ell_{i}}
is a dyadic rectangle we write
\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}=\prod_{i\in A_{r}}\mathbf{Q}_{i}^{\ell_{i}}
. (Recall from Proposition
7.2
that
\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
is a dyadic cube in
\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
for the dilations
\delta^{k_{r}}
.) We use notation and results from Sections
7
and
10
with
\mathbb{R}^{\nu_{r}}=\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
.

Let
\mathfrak{P}_{s}
denote the set of all non-empty subsets of
\{1,\ldots,s\}
. Given
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
, let
\mathbb{R}^{B}=\prod_{r\in B}\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}=\prod_{r\notin B}\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
. If
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, write
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{x}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{B^{c}})\in\mathbb{R}^{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
. In the same spirit, if
\mathbf{R}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a dyadic rectangle, write
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbf{R}_{B^{c}}
.
The Fourier transform of
\phi\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is the function
\mathcal{F}[\phi]({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-2\pi i\langle{\boldsymbol{\xi}},\mathbf{x}\rangle}\phi(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}
. Then
\mathcal{F}_{B}
and
\mathcal{F}_{B^{c}}
are the partial transforms in the variables
\mathbf{x}_{B}
and
\mathbf{x}_{B^{c}}
respectively:
\mathcal{F}_{B}[\phi]({\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{{B^{c}}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{B}}e^{-2\pi i\langle{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{B}\rangle}\phi(\mathbf{x}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{{B^{c}}})\,d\mathbf{x}_{B}
and
\mathcal{F}_{{B^{c}}}[\phi](\mathbf{x}_{B},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{{B^{c}}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}}e^{-2\pi i\langle{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{{B^{c}}},\mathbf{x}_{{B^{c}}}\rangle}\phi(\mathbf{x}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{{B^{c}}})\,d\mathbf{x}_{{B^{c}}}
.

To state the main result, fix
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{E}}
, fix a subset
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
with cardinality
|B|
, and fix a parameter
\tau>0
. Let
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbf{R}_{{B^{c}}}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}
with
\mathbf{R}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{i}^{\ell_{i}}
, and let
\mathbf{m}_{B}=(m_{r})_{r\in B}\in\mathbb{N}^{|B|}
with
1\leq m_{r}\leq\ell_{k_{r}}
for
r\in B
. Then put

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B,\mathbf{m}}

\displaystyle=2^{\mathbf{m}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{R}_{B}=\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}2^{m_{r}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{cc}_{B,\mathbf{m}}

\displaystyle=\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}(2^{m_{r}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}})^{c}.

(11.1)

It is important to note that
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{cc}_{B,\mathbf{m}}\subsetneqq(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B,\mathbf{m}})^{c}
if
s\geq 2
.

Moreover we observe that, keeping the notation introduced at the end of Section
7.4
, the enlargement by rescaling
2^{\sigma}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
in each factor subspace
\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
is made with respect to the dilations
\delta^{k_{r}}
. Since the scalar coordinates
x_{h}
of points
\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}
will intervene in the next proofs, it is convenient to introduce, for
h=1,\dots,d
, a new notation,
\lambda^{\prime}_{h}
(depending on the marked partition
S
), for the exponent of
x_{h}
in these dilations. Explicitly, if
h\in E_{i}
with
i\in A_{r}
, then

\lambda^{\prime}_{h}=\frac{\lambda_{h}}{e(k_{r},i)}.

(11.2)

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}
, let
\tau>0
,
B\in\mathcal{P}_{s}
,
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbf{R}_{{B^{c}}}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}
, and let
f\in L^{2}(R^{d})
be supported on
(2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{R}_{B})\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
, with cancellation in the coordinate
\mathbf{x}_{k_{r}}
for each
r\in B
. Let
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
be a set of finite measure. Given
\mathbf{m}_{B}=(m_{r})_{r\in B}
with
1\leq m_{r}\leq\ell_{k_{r}}
for
r\in B
, let

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}=2^{\mathbf{m}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{R}_{B}=\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}2^{m_{r}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}},\qquad\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{cc}_{B}=\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}(2^{m_{r}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}})^{c}

Then there exists
\delta>0
such that, if
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathbf{E})
is a multi-norm kernel, there is a constant
C_{\mathcal{K}}
depending on only finitely many kernel constants of
\mathcal{K}
so that

\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{cc}_{B}\times V}\big|f*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}|\mathbf{R}_{B}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|V|^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}_{B}|}\|f\|_{2}\ .

(11.3)

If
B=\{1,\dots,s\}
there is no set
V
, and no factor
|V|^{\frac{1}{2}}
in (
11.3
).

We begin with some preliminary lemmas.

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
\gamma_{0}=\max_{1\leq h\leq d}(\lambda^{\prime}_{h})^{-1}
where
\{\lambda^{\prime}_{h}\}
are the exponents in (
11.2
).
Let
\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
be a dyadic cube with center
\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}
, and let
\tau>0
. If
\mathbf{y}_{i}\in 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
and
\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}\notin 2^{\tau+1}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
, then
2^{-\gamma_{0}}|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}|\leq|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}|\leq 2^{\gamma_{0}}|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}|
.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
has scale
2^{-\ell_{r}}
. With
E_{A_{r}}=\bigcup_{i\in A_{r}}E_{i}
, write
\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}=(c_{h})_{h\in E_{A_{r}}}
,
\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}=(x_{h})_{h\in E_{A_{r}}}
and
\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}=(y_{h})_{h\in E_{A_{r}}}
. Then for each
h\in E_{A_{r}}
we have
|y_{h}-c_{h}|\leq\frac{1}{2}2^{\tau\lambda^{\prime}_{h}-\mu_{\ell_{i},h}}
and
|x_{h}-c_{h}|\geq 2^{\tau\lambda^{\prime}_{h}-\mu_{\ell_{i},h}}>2|y_{h}-c_{h}|
, so
|x_{h}-c_{h}|<2|x_{h}-y_{h}|
and
|x_{h}-y_{h}|<2|x_{h}-c_{h}|
. Since
|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}|=\max_{h\in E_{A_{r}}}|x_{h}|^{1/\lambda^{\prime}_{h}}
, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{A_{r}}
be dyadic cubes with centers
\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}
,
\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}^{\prime}
and scales
2^{-\ell_{r}}
,
2^{-\ell^{\prime}_{r}}
such that
2^{\tau+1}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}\subseteq 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{A_{r}}
.
Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}})
. There is a constant
\varepsilon>0
and for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
a constant
C_{N}
so that if
\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}\in 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
,
\mathbf{x}_{i}\notin 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{A_{r}}
and
m_{r}\in\mathbb{N}
, then

\displaystyle\big|\psi\big(2^{m_{r}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}})\big)

\displaystyle-\psi\big(2^{m_{r}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{N}\min\big\{2^{-\varepsilon(\ell_{r}-m_{r})},2^{-\varepsilon(m_{r}-\ell^{\prime}_{r})}\big\}\big[1+2^{m_{r}}|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}|\big]^{-N}

\displaystyle\leq C_{N}2^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(\ell_{r}-\ell^{\prime}_{r})}\big[1+2^{m_{r}}|\mathbf{x}_{r}-\mathbf{y}_{r}|\big]^{-N}\ .

The constants
C_{N}
only depend only on a Schwartz norm of
\psi
, of order
M_{N}
depending only on
N
, and the exponents
\lambda^{\prime}_{h}
in (
11.2
).

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
11.2
that
|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}}|\sim|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}|
.
The rapid decay of
\psi
then gives
\big|\psi\big(2^{m_{r}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}})\big)-\psi\big(2^{m_{r}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{A_{r}})\big)\big|\leq C_{N,\psi}[1+2^{m_{r}}|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}|]^{-N}
.
Since
\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}\in 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}
and
\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}\notin 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{A_{r}}
, the non-isotropic mean value theorem (see
[
13
]
) gives an additional gain of
C_{\psi}\big|2^{m_{r}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}})\big|^{\sigma}\leq C_{\psi}2^{-\epsilon(\ell_{r}-m_{r})}
. On the other hand we can use a power of
2^{m_{r}}|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}|
in the denominator to give an additional gain of
C_{\psi}\big|2^{m_{r}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}})\big|^{-\sigma}\leq C_{\psi}2^{-\epsilon(m_{r}-\ell^{\prime}_{r})}
. This gives the first estimate. The second inequality comes from comparison between minimum and geometric mean.
∎

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
f
,
B
,
\mathbf{R}
,
\mathbf{m}_{B}
be as in Proposition
11.1
(in particular,
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{L}
with
L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
in the notation of (
7.4
)). Let also
\mathbf{c}_{B}
be the center of
\mathbf{R}_{B}
,
\eta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be supported in the unit ball
B(1)
and
J\in\mathcal{L}
.
There exist constants
\mu>1
and
\nu>0
depending only on the exponents
\lambda_{h}
and the entries of the matrix
\mathbf{E}
so that if
m_{r}>\mu
for all
r\in B
and if
f*\eta^{(-J)}
does not vanish identically on
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
then
j_{i}\leq\ell_{i}-\nu\,m_{r}
for all
r\in B
and
i\in A_{r}
.

Proof.

Since
J
need not be in
\mathcal{L}_{S}
, we must work with the single components
\mathbf{x}_{i}
of
\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}
with
r\in B
. For
i\in A_{r}
we also let
m_{i}=m_{r}/e(k_{r},i)
, not necessarily an integer.
Let
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\in\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
such that
f*\eta_{J}^{(-J)}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(\mathbf{y})\eta_{J}^{(-J)}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\,d\mathbf{y}\neq 0
.
Since
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}
, for every
r\in B
there exists
i_{r}\in A_{r}
such that
|\mathbf{x}_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{i_{r}}|^{e(k_{r},i_{r})}\geq 2^{\tau+m_{r}-\ell_{k_{r}}}
, that is
|\mathbf{x}_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{i_{r}}|\geq C_{\tau}2^{m_{i_{r}}-\frac{\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i_{r})}}\geq C^{\prime}_{\tau}2^{m_{i_{r}}-\ell_{i_{r}}}
, since
L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
. On the other hand there exists
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{R}_{B}
so that
2^{J_{B}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\in 2^{\tau}\circ B^{\prime}(1)
,
B^{\prime}(1)
being the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{B}
. Thus

\displaystyle h\in E_{i_{r}}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow|y_{h}-c_{h}|^{\frac{1}{\lambda_{h}}}\leq|\mathbf{y}_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{i_{r}}|\leq 2^{\tau-\ell_{i_{r}}}\text{\quad and\quad}|x_{h}-y_{h}|^{\frac{1}{\lambda_{h}}}\leq 2^{\tau-j_{i_{r}}}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow|y_{h}-c_{h}|\leq 2^{\lambda_{h}(\tau-\ell_{i_{r}})}\text{\quad and\quad}|x_{h}-y_{h}|\leq 2^{\lambda_{h}(\tau-j_{i_{r}})}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow|x_{h}-c_{h}|\leq\big[2^{\lambda_{h}(\tau-\ell_{i_{r}})}+2^{\lambda_{h}(\tau-j_{i_{r}})}\big]

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow|\mathbf{x}_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{i_{r}}|\leq\big[2^{\lambda_{h}(\tau-\ell_{i_{r}})}+2^{\lambda_{h}(\tau-j_{i_{r}})}\big]^{\frac{1}{\lambda_{h}}}\leq C_{\tau}\big(2^{-\ell_{i_{r}}}+2^{-j_{i_{r}}}\big),

where
C
depends only on the exponents
\lambda_{h}
. Combining the two inequalities we have

\displaystyle 2^{m_{i_{r}}-\ell_{i_{r}}}\leq|\mathbf{x}_{i_{r}}-\mathbf{c}_{i_{r}}|\leq\begin{cases}2^{-\ell_{i_{r}}+c_{\tau}}&\text{if $\ell_{i_{r}}\leq j_{i_{r}}$}\\
2^{-j_{i_{r}}+c_{\tau}}&\text{if $\ell_{i_{r}}>j_{i_{r}}$}.\end{cases}

(11.4)

Let
\mu>1+2e(k_{r},i_{r})c_{\tau}
for all
r\in B
. We can exclude that for some
r
, we have
m_{r}>\mu
and
\ell_{i_{r}}\leq j_{i_{r}}
, because in this case it would follows from (
11.4
) that
m_{i_{r}}\leq c_{\tau}
and then
m_{r}=e(k_{r},i_{r})m_{i_{r}}\leq e(k_{r},i_{r})c_{\tau}<\mu
, a contradiction. Assume therefore that
m_{r}>\mu
for
r\in B
. Then, for
r\in B
,
\ell_{i_{r}}>j_{i_{r}}
and it follows from (
11.4
) that
j_{i_{r}}-\ell_{i_{r}}\leq-m_{i_{r}}+c_{\tau}<\frac{-2m_{r}+\mu-1}{2e(k_{r},i_{r})}<-\frac{m_{r}}{2e(k_{r},i_{r})}
. Now let
i\in A_{r}
. This proves the thesis for
i=i_{r}
with
\nu=\big(2e(k_{r},i_{r})\big)^{-1}
.

In order to extend the inequality to all
i\in A_{r}
, assume first that
i=k_{r}
, in case
k_{r}\neq i_{r}
. Since
J\in\mathcal{L}
we have
j_{k_{r}}\leq e(k_{r},i_{r})j_{i_{r}}+\kappa
where
\kappa
is the constant in Lemma
3.9
. Since
L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
we have
\ell_{i_{r}}=\big\lfloor\frac{\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i_{r})}\big\rfloor\leq\frac{\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i_{r})}
. Hence
j_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}\leq e(k_{r},i_{r})(j_{i_{r}}-\ell_{i_{r}})+\kappa\leq-\frac{m_{r}}{2}+\kappa
. With the further condition
\mu>4\kappa
we obtain

j_{k_{r}}-\ell_{k_{r}}\leq-\frac{m_{r}}{4}
.
Finally, for general
i\in A_{r}
, using the inequalities
j_{i}\leq e(i,k_{r})j_{k_{r}}+\kappa

and
\ell_{i}=\big\lfloor\frac{\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}\big\rfloor\geq\frac{\ell_{k_{r}}}{e(k_{r},i)}-1
, the argument is very similar.
∎

Now let
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathbf{E})
, expressed as a dyadic sum
\mathcal{K}=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{L}}\eta_{J}^{(-J)}
according to Theorem
3.11
(
4
). Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\eta_{0}=0
and that all the other
\eta_{J}
are supported in the unit ball
B(2^{\tau})
.
We split indices
J\in\mathcal{L}
as
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
according to the splitting
\mathbb{N}^{n}=\mathbb{N}^{B}\times\mathbb{N}^{{B^{c}}}
. For better clarity, we denote the entries of
J^{\prime}
as
j^{\prime}_{i}
for
i\in B
and the entries of
J^{\prime\prime}
as
j^{\prime\prime}_{i}
for
i\notin B
. If
L\in\mathcal{L}
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{L}

\displaystyle=\Big\{J=(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{L}:\text{$f*\eta_{J}^{(-J)}\not\equiv 0$ on $\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}$}\big\},

(11.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\big\{J^{\prime}:\exists\,J=(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{L}_{L}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{L,J^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\big\{J^{\prime\prime}:(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{L}_{L}\big\}\text{ \,if $J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{J^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{L,J^{\prime}}}\eta_{(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})}^{(-(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}))}.

Remarks 11.5
.

(1)

It follows from Lemma
11.4
that
\mathcal{L}_{L}
is a finite set, and its cardinality grows at most polynomially in
|L|
. Consequently the sets
\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}_{L,J^{\prime}}^{\prime\prime}
are also finite sets.

(2)

Since
\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}
is a finite sum, it is a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-functions with compact support.

(3)

If
\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\neq 0
then
|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}|\leq 2^{-j_{k_{r}}}
for each
r\in B
.

Lemma 11.6
.

Let
\mathbf{h}\in F(B)
and let
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})=\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})
.
Then
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}
is a multi-norm kernel on the space
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
relative to the matrix
\mathbf{E}_{B}=\big(e(j,k)\big)_{j,k\in B}
, with the constants in equations (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) replaced by
C_{\alpha,N}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j^{\prime}_{k_{r}}\lambda_{h_{r}}+\sum_{i\in B}j^{\prime}_{i}q_{i}}
so that, for all derivatives
\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}}
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}}^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha,N}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j^{\prime}_{k_{r}}(\lambda_{h_{r}}+q_{k_{r}})}\prod\nolimits_{i\in B^{c}}N_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})^{-q_{i}-[\![\alpha_{i}]\!]}\big(1+\|\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}\|\big)^{-N},

and

\big|\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}[\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}]({\boldsymbol{\xi}}^{\prime\prime})\big|\leq C\,2^{\sum_{r\in B}j^{\prime}_{k_{r}}(\lambda_{h_{r}}+q_{k_{r}})}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{J^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{L,J^{\prime}}}\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\big[\eta_{(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})}^{(-J^{\prime},-J^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\big]

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{J^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{L,J^{\prime}}}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j^{\prime}_{k_{r}}\lambda_{h_{r}}}\big(\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\eta_{(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})}\big)^{(-J^{\prime}-,J^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{J^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}_{L,J^{\prime}}}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j_{k_{r}}\lambda_{h_{r}}+\sum_{i\in B}j_{i}q_{i}}\big(\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\eta_{(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})}\big)^{(0,-J^{\prime\prime})}(2^{J^{\prime}}\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

Since the functions

\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\eta_{(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})}(2^{J^{\prime}}\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\cdot)
, depending on the parameters
\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
, are uniformly bounded in every Schwartz norm and have the required cancellations, the conclusion follows easily (notice that the factor
2^{J^{\prime}}
plays in favour of uniformity in
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
11.1
.

We can assume
b=|B|<s
since, as will be clear from the proof, the case
B=\{1,\dots,s\}
is simpler.
The cancellations of
f
allow us to express it as a sum of derivatives. Lemma
11.6
shows that for each
\mathbf{h}\in F(B)
there exists
g_{\mathbf{h}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
supported on
2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
so that

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{h}\in F}\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}g_{\mathbf{h}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\text{ with }

\displaystyle\|g_{\mathbf{h}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}2^{-\lambda_{h_{r}}\ell^{\prime}_{k_{r}}}\|f\|_{2}.

To prove the Proposition we can assume that
f
consists of a single term in the above sum: thus assume
there exists
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
supported on
2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
, and for each
r\in B
there is an index
h_{r}\in E_{k_{r}}
, so that

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}g(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})=\Big(\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}\partial_{x_{h_{r}}}\Big)g(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}),

(11.6)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\,2^{-\sum_{r\in B}\lambda_{h_{r}}\ell^{\prime}_{k_{r}}}\|f\|_{2}.

If
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}=\prod_{r\in B}\big(2^{m_{r}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}\big)^{c}
then for each
r\in B
there exists
i_{r}\in A_{r}
so that
\mathbf{x}_{i_{r}}\notin 2^{m_{r}+\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}^{\ell_{i}}
, and therefore
|\mathbf{x}_{i_{r}}|>2^{\tau+m_{r}-\ell_{i_{r}}-1}
. Now let
(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\in\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}\times V
. Using (
11.5
) and (
11.6
) and integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle f*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{L}_{L}}f*\eta_{J}^{(-J)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})=\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}f*\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})

(11.7)

\displaystyle=\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{L}}\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}g*\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})=\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{L}}g*\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}).

Thus from (
11.7
)

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{c}_{B}\times V}\big|f*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}\leq\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{c}_{B}\times V}\big|g*\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}\int_{2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}}\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}}\Big[\int_{V}\Big|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})\big|d\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}\Big]\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}\int_{2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}}\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}}\Big[\int_{V}\Big|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})\Big|^{2}d\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}\int_{2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}}\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}^{cc}}\big\|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\,\cdot\,)*^{\prime\prime}\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}})}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime},

where
*^{\prime\prime}
is convolution in the
\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}
-variables. Let
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})=\partial^{b}_{\mathbf{h}}\mathcal{K}_{L,J^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})
. According to Lemma
11.6
,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}
is a multi-norm kernel on
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
with constants independent of
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
and equal to those of
\mathcal{K}
multiplied by the factor
2^{\sum_{r\in B}j_{k_{r}}\lambda_{h_{r}}+\sum_{i\in B}j_{i}^{\prime}q_{i}}
. Also
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})=0
unless
|\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}_{A_{r}}^{\prime}|\leq 2^{\tau-j^{\prime}_{k_{r}}}
for all
r\in B
. Then using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Remark
11.5
(
3
), and the
L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{B^{c}})
-boundedness of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{c}_{B}\times V}\big|f*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}\int_{2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{B}}\big\|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\,\cdot\,)*^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{h},J^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}(\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}})}}{\mathbf{1}}_{2^{-J^{\prime}}\circ B(1)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j_{k_{r}}^{\prime}\lambda_{h_{r}}+\sum_{i\in B}j_{i}^{\prime}q_{i}}\int_{2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}}\big\|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}})}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{B}}{\mathbf{1}}_{2^{-J^{\prime}}\circ B(1)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\Big]\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{K},\tau}\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j_{k_{r}}^{\prime}\lambda_{h_{r}}}\int_{2^{\tau}\mathbf{R}_{B}}\big\|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}})}\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{K},\tau}\big|\mathbf{R}_{B}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j_{k_{r}}^{\prime}\lambda_{h_{r}}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{B}}\big\|g(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\cdot)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}})}\,d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=C_{\mathcal{K},\tau}\big|\mathbf{R}_{B}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}2^{\sum_{r\in B}j_{k_{r}}^{\prime}\lambda_{h_{r}}}\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{K},\tau}\big|\mathbf{R}_{B}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{2}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}2^{-\sum_{r\in B}\lambda_{h_{r}}(\ell^{\prime}_{k_{r}}-j^{\prime}_{k_{r}})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{K},\tau}\big|\mathbf{R}_{B}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|V\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{2}\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}}2^{-\varepsilon|L^{\prime}-J^{\prime}|},

for some
\varepsilon>0
. We have used that, since
J\in\mathcal{L}
and
L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}
,
\ell^{\prime}_{i}-j^{\prime}_{i}\leq(\ell^{\prime}_{k_{r}}-j^{\prime}_{k_{r}})/e(k_{r},i)
for every
i\in A_{r}
. By Lemma
11.4
, for
J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}^{\prime}
we have
|L^{\prime}-J^{\prime}|\geq c|\mathbf{m}_{B}|
and this concludes the proof.
∎

12.
Atoms belong to
\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{E}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

In this section we show that the multi-norm atoms
a
in Definition
9.1
belong to the Hardy space
\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{E}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We remark that if
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}
then an
(S,\tau)
-atom is a Chang-Fefferman product atom relative to the decomposition
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\prod_{r=1}^{s}\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
with dilations
\delta^{k_{r}}
in the
A_{r}
-factor (or a Coifman-Weiss atom if
s=1
, see Remarks
9.2
.) It follows that the ‘partial’ square function
S_{\Psi,S}a=\big(\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}_{S}}|a*\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. It is thanks to the extra restrictions on a multi-norm atom (supporting rectangles of pre-atoms in
\mathbb{D}_{S}
and stronger cancellation assumptions) that we obtain
S_{\Psi,S^{\prime}}a\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
also for the other marked partitions
S^{\prime}
.

Theorem 12.1
.

There is a constant
C>0
so that
\|a\|_{\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{E}}^{1}}\leq C
for all multi-norm atoms
a
.

By Corollary
8.10
, Theorem
12.1
is a direct consequence of the following Proposition.

Proposition 12.2
.

Let
S{=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{E}}^{*}
and let
\tau>1
. If
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}(\mathbf{E})
is a multi-norm kernel, there is a constant
C_{\mathcal{K}}>0
so that
\|a*\mathcal{K}\|_{1}\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}
for any
(S,\tau)
-atom
a
.
C_{\mathcal{K}}
depends on finitely many of the kernel constants of
\mathcal{K}
in (
3.21
) and (
3.22
).

Proof.

Let
a
be an
(S,\tau)
-atom with
a=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}a_{\mathbf{R}}
and
\Omega={\rm sh}(\mathcal{U})\subseteq 2^{\tau}\circ B(1)
. The argument depends on the number
s
of dotted entries in
S
and is straightforward if
s\leq 1
. If
s=0
, the
L^{2}
-boundedness of convolution with
\mathcal{K}
and the fact that
a*\mathcal{K}
is supported on a ball of bounded radius shows that
\|a*\mathcal{K}\|_{1}\leq C\|a*\mathcal{K}\|_{2}\leq C^{\prime}\|a\|_{2}\leq C^{\prime}
. If
s=1
and
a
is supported in
2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{R}
with
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{D}_{S}
,
split
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big|a*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}
into the sum of the integral over
2^{1+\tau}\circ\mathbf{R}
and the integral over its complement. For the first integral, again apply the
L^{2}
-boundedness of convolution by
\mathcal{K}
. For the integral over the complement it is sufficient to use Proposition
11.1
with
\delta=0
and
m=1
to obtain
\int_{(2^{1+\tau}\circ\mathbf{R})^{c}}\big|a*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}|\mathbf{R}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}
.

Now let
S=\big\{(A_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(A_{s},k_{s})\big\}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{E}}
with
s\geq 2
and let
\tau>0
. An
(S,\tau)
-atom
a
is associated with an open set
\Omega\subset 2^{\tau}\circ\mathbf{Q}
where
\mathbf{Q}
a unit cube in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
a(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}a_{\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{x})
where
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathbb{D}_{S}(\Omega)
is a family of dyadic rectangles. The functions
\{a_{\mathbf{R}}:\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}\}
are pre-atoms as in part (
3
) of Definition
9.1
. Fix a complete N-string
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{N})
as Definition
10.1
. If
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}
let
\mathbf{R}^{(j)}
and
\mathbf{m}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})=\big(m_{1}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R}),\dots,m_{s}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})\big)\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
for
0\leq j\leq N
be the sequence of enlargements and multi-indices defined in equation (
10.3
). Let
\mathfrak{P}_{s}
be the family of all non-empty subsets of
\{1,\ldots,s\}
. If
\mathbf{R}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Q}_{i}\in\mathcal{U}
then
\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}}=\prod_{i\in A_{r}}\mathbf{Q}_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}
is a dyadic cube with respect to the dilations
\delta^{k_{r}}
. Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{A_{r}}

\displaystyle=2^{m_{r}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{A_{r}},

(12.1)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\circ\mathbf{R}=\prod\nolimits_{r=1}^{s}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{A_{r}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}2^{\mathbf{m}^{(N)}(\mathbf{R})}\circ\mathbf{R}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}.

From Lemma
10.3
we have
|\widehat{\Omega}|\leq C|\Omega|
. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the
L^{2}
-boundedness of convolution with
\mathcal{K}
, and the
L^{2}
normalization of atoms, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{\Omega}}\big|a*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}\leq\|a*\mathcal{K}\|_{L^{2}}|\widehat{\Omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}|\widehat{\Omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a\|_{2}\leq C|\widehat{\Omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C.

Thus to prove the proposition when
s\geq 2
we must show that
\int_{\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}\big|a*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}
, and to proceed we partition
\widehat{\Omega}^{c}
into regions indexed by sets
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
. We recall the following notation from Section
10.2
. If
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
write
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}=\big(\prod_{r\in B}\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}\big)\times\big(\prod_{r\notin B}\mathbb{R}^{A_{r}}\big)
, and if
\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}
write
\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{R}_{B}\times\mathbf{R}_{B^{c}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
. We define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B}

\displaystyle=\prod_{r\in B}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{A_{r}}

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B})^{cc}

\displaystyle=\prod_{r\in B}\big(\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{A_{r}}\big)^{c}.

(12.2)

The symbols
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}
denote dyadic rectangles in
\mathbb{R}^{B}
and
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
, and we use similar notation for multi-indices
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}
and
\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B^{c}}
. Given
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}
and
0\leq j\leq N
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}:\mathbf{m}_{B}^{(j)}(\mathbf{R})=\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\Big\},

(12.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{B}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{B}:\exists\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}\text{ with }\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\Big\},

and if
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{B}
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}:\mathbf{R}_{B}=\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\Big\}

(12.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}:\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\Big\}.

With
|B|
-tuples
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}
ordered component-wise, we also define

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\mathcal{U}^{j,\mathbf{n}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{B}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\mathcal{U}_{B}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\mathcal{V}^{j,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}).

(12.5)

With this notation in place we can now proceed with the proof.

The inclusion-exclusion formula gives

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\mathbf{x})={\mathbf{1}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}}(\mathbf{x})+\sum\nolimits_{B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}}(-1)^{|B|+1}\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}{\mathbf{1}}_{(\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{A_{r}})^{c}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}})
. Then since
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\subseteq\widehat{\Omega}
we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{1}}_{\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}}(-1)^{|B|+1}\prod\nolimits_{r\in B}{\mathbf{1}}_{(\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{A_{r}})^{c}}(\mathbf{x}_{A_{r}}){\mathbf{1}}_{\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}(\mathbf{x})

(12.6)

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}}(-1)^{|B|+1}{\mathbf{1}}_{(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}}(\mathbf{x}){\mathbf{1}}_{\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}(\mathbf{x}).

Thus if
\displaystyle I_{B}=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}\int_{[(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}]\cap\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}a_{\mathbf{R}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}
it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}a*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}a_{\mathbf{R}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}=\sum_{B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}}(-1)^{|B|+1}\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}\int_{[(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{B})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}]\cap\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}a_{\mathbf{R}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}}(-1)^{|B|+1}I_{B},

and to prove the proposition it suffices to show that
|I_{B}|\leq C
for every
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
.

We argue by “reverse induction” on the cardinality of
B
, and we consider first the case of
B_{0}=\{1,\ldots,s\}
. From (
12.3
) we have the partition
\mathcal{U}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}}\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}}
. Using the inclusion
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\subseteq\widehat{\Omega}
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and Propositions
11.1
and
10.2
we have

\displaystyle|I_{B_{0}}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}\int_{(\widehat{\mathbf{R}})^{cc}\cap\widehat{\Omega}^{c}}a_{\mathbf{R}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\,d\mathbf{x}\Big|\leq\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}}\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{cc}}\big|a_{\mathbf{R}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}}}\int_{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{cc}}\big|a_{\mathbf{R}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}|}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}}}\big|\mathbf{R}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a_{\mathbf{R}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}|}\Big[\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}}}\big|\mathbf{R}\big|\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\sum_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}}}\|a_{\mathbf{R}}\|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}|}\big(1+|\mathbf{m}|\big)^{p/2}|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\Omega\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim 1.

It remains to prove the estimate
|I_{B}|\lesssim 1
for proper subsets of
\{1,\ldots,s\}
.
For every
B\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
we have the partition

\mathcal{U}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
,
which we use to group together the
a_{\mathbf{R}}
. Define

a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})}a_{\mathbf{R}},

so that

a=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}\,a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}
.
For
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}
fixed, if
\mathbf{R}_{1},\,\mathbf{R}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
then the two dyadic enlargements
2^{\mathbf{m}(\mathbf{R}_{1})}\mathbf{R}_{1},\,2^{\mathbf{m}(\mathbf{R}_{2})}\mathbf{R}_{2}
have the same
B
-component
2^{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
, and the same is true for the enlargement by dilations. Thus
(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{1})_{B}=(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{2})_{B}
. We denote this last set by
\widehat{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}
. If now we put

\displaystyle S^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})=\int_{({\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}}\big|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\,d\mathbf{x}^{\prime\prime}

(12.7)

then

\displaystyle|I_{B}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}\int_{({\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}^{*})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}}\big|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}=\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}S^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}).

(12.8)

We show that the sum
\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}S^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
in (
12.8
) is bounded by a constant depending only on
\mathcal{K}
,
\mathcal{U}
,
\Omega

for sets
B
with
|B|=b<s
assuming the inductive hypothesis that the same holds for all
H
with
|H|>b
.

Now suppose
b<s
and that
\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}S^{H}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
is bounded for all subsets
H\in\mathfrak{P}_{s}
with
|H|>b
. Fix a pair
(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
with
\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}
. We split the integral in (
12.7
) into two parts by decomposing
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
into two pieces. Replacing
\mathcal{U}
with
\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}
we apply the definitions in (
12.3
) and (
12.4
) and introduce enlargements
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
and a family of rectangles
\widetilde{\Omega}=\big\{\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}:\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\big\}
. We also fix a complete
N^{\prime}
-string
\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}\in(B^{c})^{N^{\prime}}
and define
\widehat{\widetilde{\Omega}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
as in Lemma
10.3
. Then

\displaystyle S^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\big((\widehat{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})^{cc}\times\widetilde{\Omega}^{(j)}}\big|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}+\int_{(\widehat{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}})^{cc}\times(\widetilde{\Omega}^{(j)})^{c}}\big|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}

(12.9)

\displaystyle=S^{B}_{1}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})+S^{B}_{2}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}).

The inequality (
10.6
) holds true,
a fortiori
, with
\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}
,
\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}_{B})
in place of
\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}
,
\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
.

We first deal with
\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}S^{B}_{1}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
.
Applying Proposition
11.1
with
f=a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}
and using the inequality
\big|\widehat{\widetilde{\Omega}}\big|\leq C|\widetilde{\Omega}|=C|\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})|
we obtain

\displaystyle S^{B}_{1}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{K}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\widehat{\widetilde{\Omega}}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|}\big\|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}=C_{\mathcal{K}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|{\rm sh}\big(\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\big)\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|}\big\|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}.

Summing over
\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
and using Proposition
10.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}S^{B}_{1}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\leq\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}2^{-\delta|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|{\rm sh}\big(\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}2^{-2\delta|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}|\mathbf{R}^{\prime}|\big|{\rm sh}\big(\mathcal{V}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\big|\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}\big\|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\Omega\big|\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}2^{-2\delta|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|}\big(1+|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|)^{p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}\big\|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\Omega|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim 1.

This takes care of the sum
\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}S_{1}^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
.

To deal with
\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}S^{B}_{2}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
we decompose integration over
\big(\widehat{\widetilde{\Omega}}\big)^{c}
into a sum of terms, depending on nonempty subsets
G
of
B^{c}
and analogous to the
I_{B}
in (
12.8
). We denote by
\mathcal{P}_{B^{c}}
the collection of these sets
G
. For
G\in\mathcal{P}_{B^{c}}
, we set
H=B\cup G
and
H^{c}=\{1,\dots,s\}\setminus H=B^{c}\setminus G
. We write
\mathbb{R}^{d}=\mathbb{R}^{B}\times\mathbb{R}^{G}\times\mathbb{R}^{H^{c}}
, and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
as
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{x}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{B^{c}})=(\mathbf{x}_{B},\mathbf{x}_{G},\mathbf{x}_{H^{c}})
(if
G=B^{c}
the third component is not present). We also denote rectangles in
\mathbb{R}^{G}
by
\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}
and
G
-tuples of nonegative integers by
\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}
. Using the inclusion-exclusion formula (
12.6
) in the
\mathbb{R}^{B^{c}}
variables to obtain the inequality

\big|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|{\mathbf{1}}_{\big(\widehat{\widetilde{\Omega}}\big)^{c}}(\mathbf{x}_{B^{c}})\leq\sum\nolimits_{G\in\mathcal{P}_{B^{c}}}\big|a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|{\mathbf{1}}_{(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{G})^{cc}}(\mathbf{x}_{G}).

Since
\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})
decomposes as the disjoint union

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime})=\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{G}}\bigcup\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})}_{H}}\mathcal{U}^{N,(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})}(\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}),

we can write

a_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}=\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{G}}\sum\nolimits_{\mathbb{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})}_{H}}\,\,a_{(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}}.

Thus

\displaystyle S_{2}^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})\leq\sum_{G\in\mathcal{P}_{B^{c}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{G}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})}_{B\cup G}}\int_{(\widehat{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{H^{c}}}\big|a_{(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}.

(12.10)

Summing over
\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{B}}

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,\mathbf{m}^{\prime}}_{B}}S_{2}^{B}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\sum\nolimits_{H\supsetneqq B}\sum\nolimits_{(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{H}}\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime})}_{H}}\int_{(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{H})^{cc}\times\mathbb{R}^{H^{c}}}\big|a_{(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime}}*\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x})\big|\,d\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{H\supsetneqq B}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{H}}\sum\nolimits_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{U}^{N,(\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime\prime})}_{H}}S_{2}^{H}(\mathbf{m}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbf{R}^{\prime\prime\prime})\Big).

Each expression in parentheses is uniformly bounded by the inductive hypothesis, and this completes the proof.
∎

13.
Further comments

The study of square functions and Hardy spaces developed in this paper is far from being complete. This is due in part to a choice, in order to limit the size of the paper, in part to the fact that certain aspects of the theory would require further thoughts.

1.

The multi-norm Littlewood-Paley decomposition presented in Sections
2
and
3
is local for a good reason. If we extend the content of Section
3
to take into account all scales, we immediately realize that large and small scales, either in space or in frequency, behave differently and independently one from the other. To begin with, a dyadic annulus
\widehat{N}_{i}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\sim 2^{p}
with
p>0
cannot intersect another dyadic annulus
\widehat{N}_{j}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})\sim 2^{q}
with
q<0
. Moreover the rules that determine which intersections of dyadic annuli are different in the two ranges. Though the “far-from-local” part of the theory may also have some interest, we focus on the local part, which is more natural also because, in principle, it could be extended to non-translation invariant contexts, such as in
[
29
, Ch. 12, 13]
.

2.

We believe that an extension of
L^{1}
-equivalence to
L^{p}
-equivalence for
p<1
and close to 1 is possible using the techniques of Section
5
.

3.

We do not consider characterizations of
\mathbf{h}^{1}_{\mathbf{E}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
via maximal operators. Natural candidates as “multi-norm maximal operators” are those of the form

M_{\varphi}f(\mathbf{x})=\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}\in\Gamma({\mathbf{E}})}\big|f*\varphi^{(-{\mathbf{t}})}(\mathbf{x})\big|\ ,

with
\int\varphi=1
, as well as grand-maximal analogues.

4.

We do not know whether the results of Section
4.2
still hold removing the assumption that the
\Psi_{L}
are tensor products, but keeping the other conditions, that is, uniform boundedness in Schwartz norms, cancellation in each variable
\mathbf{x}_{i}
with
i\in D_{S}
and
\sum_{L\in\mathcal{L}}\Psi_{L}^{(-L)}=\delta_{\mathbf{0}}
.
More generally, we do not know whether it is possible to obtain, in a simple form, a larger class of mutually
L^{1}
-equivalent square functions which includes those of both tensor- and convolution-type.

5.

In this paper we do not consider continuous-parameter square functions that are natural counterparts of those studied Sections
5
-
7.5
. In some cases, it is not hard to obtain their
L^{1}
-equivalence with those in this paper, adapting the arguments of Section
5
. This is the case with a
g
-function of tensor type depending on the choice, for
i=1,\dots,n
, of
\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}})
with
\int\varphi_{i}=1
and of the form

g(f)(\mathbf{x})=\big(\big|f*\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{E}}}\int_{F_{S}}\big|f*\Psi_{S}^{(-{\mathbf{t}})}(\mathbf{x})\big|^{2}\,d\sigma_{S}({\mathbf{t}})\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
Here
F_{S}
is the face (
3.11
) associated to
S\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{E}}
and
\sigma_{S}
is the Hausdorff measure on
F_{S}
. Moreover,
\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{x})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
and
\Psi_{S}(\mathbf{x})=\Big(\prod_{i\in D_{S}}\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod_{i\not\in D_{S}}\varphi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Big)
where
\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=(d/dt)_{|_{t=1}}\varphi^{(-t)}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
.

6.

There are close relations between multi-norm and flag structures. The paper
[
29
]
was initially motivated by the remarkable observation that a singular kernel that is simultaneously a flag kernel for two “opposite” flags is singular only at the origin and locally satisfies the conditions of a multi-norm kernel. Heuristically, flag structures can be regarded as “limits” of (special) multi-norm structures. Considering, for instance, the flag
\mathbb{R}_{x_{1}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}_{x_{1},x_{2}}\subset\cdots\mathbb{R}^{n}
endowed with isotropic dilations, the corresponding flag-seminorms
N_{i}=\max\big\{|x_{i}|,|x_{i+1}|,\dots,|x_{n}|\big\}
and
\widehat{N}_{j}=\max\big\{|\xi_{1}|,|\xi_{2}|,\dots,|\xi_{j}|\big\}
are the limits as
\nu\to\infty
of the norms (
2.5
) and associated to the standard matrices

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}_{\nu}=\begin{pmatrix}1&1&1&1&\cdots\\
\nu&1&1&1&\cdots\\
\nu^{2}&\nu&1&1&\cdots\\
\nu^{3}&\nu^{2}&\nu&1&\cdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\end{pmatrix}\ \qquad(\nu>1)\ ,

The Littlewood-Paley decomposition of the flag frequency space, constructed in analogy with Section
3.3
, consists of rectangles depending on parameters
L=(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}

with
\ell_{1}\leq\ell_{2}\leq\cdots\leq\ell_{n}
. Fixing one such
L
and grouping together the consecutive entries that are equal, we obtain a string
\{I_{1},\dots,I_{s}\}
of consecutive subintervals of
\{1,\dots,n\}
. Setting
k_{r}=\min I_{r}
(the dotted entry in
I_{r}
), the rectangle at scale
L
consists of the points
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that, for all
r=1,\dots,s
,

|\xi_{k_{r}}|\sim 2^{\ell_{k_{r}}}\ \text{ and }\ |\xi_{j}|\lesssim 2^{\ell_{j}}=2^{\ell_{k_{r}}}\text{ if }j\in I_{r}\setminus\{k_{r}\}\ .

We believe that analysis of flag structure with arbitrary number of factors can be developed along the lines of the present paper, extending results that are known with two factors and, at the same time, acquiring a more structured point of view.

7.

It is likely that large part of our results can be extended to homogeneous nilpotent groups, with a standard matrix
{\mathbf{E}}
that is doubly monotone according to
[
29
, Def. 9.2]
. The main obstacle to face is the lack of commutativity and the fact that not all dilations can be automorphisms. Indeed, matters are much simpler in special situations, like in the Phong-Stein case of the Heisenberg group
H_{n}\cong\mathbb{C}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
endowed with isotropic and parabolic dilations, provided the square functions involve convolution with Schwartz functions
\Psi_{L}
that are radial in the
\mathbb{C}^{n}
variable.
This last context has been studied by various authors in connection with flag structures
[
6
,
23
]
.
